It is the **one writer of `docs/compat.md` in this phase**. h2, DTLS and WebSocket submit bounded-window, version-rule and compressed-single-frame/Fragmentizer row text through their notes and DONE reports; presentation and executable gates lands all rows in one commit. Providing row text is not permission for those consumers to edit the shared document.

Shared declarations are frozen serially, never edited concurrently with their consumers. All addon-shared state stays under dispatch; no network I/O, wait for connection owners, or unrelated lock is held under dispatch. Every new addon uses upstream's `Name()` and has a registration test alongside the entire default set.

**Shared receipts, snapshots and injection**: one connection Receive owner demultiplexes by stream ID to one flow owner per exchange. The routing identity includes the endpoint/connection identity as well as stream ID, because two upstream connections can each use stream 1. Freeze ordered HEADERS, informational responses, DATA, trailers, END_STREAM, stream reset and connection-error events; the flow owner drives HttpStream, which remains the sole owner of body-limit and streaming decisions. Reject PUSH_PROMISE (no invented pushed flows), acknowledge SETTINGS and respect peer concurrent-stream, frame, header-table and window limits. The h2 adapter lives in httplayer; independent internal/h2 types prevent a package cycle.

Endpoint Send retains the existing **blocking-to-consumption, borrowed-slice** contract: no mutable DATA slice retained after return, and success means all transformed output for that event was written to the peer socket or deliberately dropped, not accepted by a queue. Each flow owner may wait on its send; connection Receive continues independently, with bounded queues and cancellation waking sends. A stream receives an original-byte receipt charged once for the DATA flow-controlled length **including padding**, independent of transformed output length. Buffered body consumption completes after append to the flow body; streaming consumption completes only after **all** transformed output is written or deliberately dropped. Expansion, drop and delayed StreamFunc output work in both request and response directions without granting credit for queued output. Cancellation/RST/GOAWAY/error releases chunks and reservations once, invalidates outstanding receipts and wakes senders; it sends no stream WINDOW_UPDATE on a closed stream.

Extend `layer.Snapshot` with cloned UDP-last-message and HTTPFlow.WebSocket last-message/data fields, including `closed_by_client`, close code/reason and `timestamp_end`. Extend Handler Inject with cloned `*udp.Message` and `*websocket.Message` variants (HTTPFlow identity for WebSocket), identity/type/direction checks and bounded queues; freeze oversize/full/closed error semantics with the consumers. One flow owner receives both directions and injections, publishes/appends via FireFunc, then reads snapshots; socket readers never mutate live flows. This is also the path used by drop/kill/intercept tests, not a direct call to the layer bypassing Handler.

**gows frame/relay contract** (reuses its existing header codec, WithBuffered and extension machinery):
1. Distinct frame/relay mode mutually exclusive with ReadMessage/NextReader/NextWriter/Serve; message-mode defaults and conformance unchanged.
2. Freeze read/write signatures and ownership. Frame results expose opcode, FIN, relevant RSV bits, payload and wire-compressed-versus-decoded status. Payload is borrowed until the next ReadFrame or Abort; the adapter clones it. `Frame.Compressed` derives from the opening RSV1 and Conn carries it to continuations; false on controls or without negotiated compression, illegal RSV1 is a ProtocolError. Writes do not retain borrowed payload after documented completion.
3. Every Ping/Pong/Close is observable: no automatic Pong, swallowed Pong, close echo, hidden drain or unsolicited controls. Validate control FIN/length, opcode, role masking, close code and UTF-8 reason. A valid Close returns `(Frame, nil)` with parsed body available through existing CloseError/helper; malformed close returns ProtocolError. After close, data is a protocol error; transport EOF yields typed peer-closed status, distinct from protocol/transport failure.
4. Mask by endpoint role with fresh keys; serialize complete frame writes; explicit opcode/FIN and zero-length fragments, with controls between continuations. Uncompressed boundary preservation is not a buffer-size hint.
5. Decode inbound compression and encode hook-edited plaintext using the opposite side's negotiated parameters, with separate inbound/outbound dictionaries, window bits and takeover flags; correct resets/trailer removal/RSV1 placement, never compressed controls. **Approved DecodeFrame contract**: non-final compressed frames return `(nil, false, nil)`, FIN returns whole plaintext/complete=true; uncompressed returns payload/complete=FIN. Call it in order for every compressed data frame, never for interleaved controls. Decoded size is bounded by Conn's read limit; overflow returns ProtocolError close code 1009 and frees buffers. Buffered decode does not claim a DEFLATE fragment-to-fragment correspondence; the Fragmentizer deviation is in deliverable 2. WriteFrame compression is selected on the opening fragment; inconsistent continuation compression and opening data during fragmentation are typed usage errors, controls remain legal/uncompressed.
6. HTTP upgrade validates actual request/response extension parameters through existing gows machinery, using approved `ParseCompression(offer, response []string) (CompressionParams, bool, error)` with one value per repeated header line and bool=negotiated. Reject unsolicited/unsupported extensions, duplicate/out-of-range parameters and construct each side explicitly. Test different direction parameters and multi-message compressed/uncompressed mixtures. gows's Dialer extension-validation fix lands as a separate tested commit after its contract.
7. WithBuffered owns/copies pre-read bytes exactly once; test complete/partial frame prefixes after 101 in one read, including masked payload prefixes and compressed fragments.
8. `Abort()` abruptly closes transport with no control write, is idempotent and safe concurrently with blocked reads/writes, freeing buffers exactly once. Conn.Close retains message-mode semantics and returns ErrConnMode in frame mode. Deadline/Abort interrupts I/O and partial-write failures terminate predictably; data after close is rejected while correct relay-owned control completion is allowed. No goroutine leaks or hidden close drain.
9. Conformance: observable raw Ping/Pong with no replies; exact fragmented text/binary/empty uncompressed frames; role violations; absent/valid/invalid close bodies; continuation/control interleaving; compressed fragmented decode/re-encode with/without takeover and differing windows; buffered partial frames; canceled blocked I/O; unchanged existing message-mode tests. Publish exact API and measured results with the dependency commit.

**Carried work and ownership**: HTTP integration owns h1 head and h2 preface/HEADERS read deadlines and the replay loaders' flowio/har `MaxEntries` bound alongside removal of h2 replay gates; DTLS owns ClientHello/head deadlines. A head deadline is separate from the idle watchdog and must expire despite trickled incomplete bytes, with cancellation/connection cleanup tests. Freeze timeout configuration at the shared contract review rather than inventing an upstream option. Gates/cleanup owns the filter/regex anchor-free capture-table check, typed capacity errors and class builder concatenation. Windows privfile ACLs/check-use and the privileged filesystem CI job stay in the next phase, not this network gate.

Deliverables
1. `go:internal/h2` plus the httplayer-owned adapter/driver: **two independent h2 endpoints per proxied connection** (client-facing server endpoint, upstream-facing client endpoint), each with its own SETTINGS, connection and stream windows, stream state table and GOAWAY handling; HEADERS/CONTINUATION assembled by the relay and decoded with its own `hpack.Decoder` (not `ReadMetaHeaders`), one `hpack.Encoder` per direction; DATA re-framed to the peer's `MAX_FRAME_SIZE`. **Advertised SETTINGS**: `MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS` 100, `INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE` 1 MiB, `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` 2^17 (upstream uses 2^31−1 windows and 2^17 frames, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http_h2.py:50-67`). **Bounded window policy** (the h2 engine supplies its deviation row to the `docs/compat.md` owner, presentation and executable gates; deviation from upstream's immediate acknowledgement, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http2.py:238-240`; confirmed by the user): the connection starts with a 128 MiB receive window (enlarged from the RFC initial 65,535 bytes by WINDOW_UPDATE), returned promptly for each valid DATA frame read off the socket, independently of stream consumption; connection credit does not authorize extra stream credit. Each receive endpoint/direction has its **own** 128 MiB stream-grant budget; these are not one combined full-duplex cap. Original-byte receipts, including DATA padding, follow the shared contract above. A stream's window is returned when the stream **consumes** the data, which means: for a streamed message (`stream_large_bodies`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:631-649`), the original chunk's full transformed output has been written to the downstream peer's socket or deliberately dropped (both request and response StreamFunc paths); queue acceptance is never consumption, and bytes that have left the receive buffer but still sit in the opposite endpoint's send buffer (`BufferedH2Connection` semantics, below) are still charged to the same Σ counter; for a message buffered for its hooks, the bytes have been appended to the flow's body buffer, which is the flow's content and is bounded by `stream_large_bodies`/`body_size_limit` exactly as on HTTP/1 (`:568-650`), not by this budget (counting only the downstream write there would deadlock every buffered body larger than its stream window, because nothing is sent downstream before the `response` hook); a 128 MiB budget per receive endpoint/direction bounds the **sum of granted (unconsumed) stream windows**: 100 MiB is reserved for the 100 initial windows, and a stream may grow beyond 1 MiB (up to 16 MiB) only by drawing on the 28 MiB headroom, first come first served; **growth trigger**: when the stream's consumer has consumed half of the stream's current window since the window was last set, the window doubles, capped at 16 MiB, or grows by the remaining headroom when less than the doubling increment is left; growth is drawn from the headroom only; reset the consumed-since-set counter when the half-window threshold triggers a growth evaluation, including partial or denied growth, so one consumption receipt cannot repeatedly trigger growth when headroom reopens. Returned stream credit replaces consumed original input credit; it does not charge transformed output. Growth returns to headroom when the stream closes, and the reserved 1 MiB returns when the stream slot is freed; so the invariant is `Σ granted stream windows ≤ 128 MiB` at all times and a stalled or intercepted stream can never consume more than its granted window; for streamed messages, **original received DATA** is bounded by 128 MiB per receive direction (both receive and handoff queues stay charged), and a stalled stream stalls only itself while the shared TCP reader continues. This invariant does not bound arbitrary memory allocated by an addon or StreamFunc expansion; transformed output uses bounded send queues, and the unchanged 160 MiB heap scenario below uses identity streaming with fixed peer buffers; a buffered body is the flow's content and is bounded only by `body_size_limit`, which is unset by default, as upstream's is (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:176-178`); the endpoint owner maintains Σ without cross-owner locks, asserts `Σ ≤ 128 MiB` on every grant/release transition, and publishes a synchronized immutable snapshot and maximum for tests; observations do not race-read its counter; **chunk hand-off**: each stream's receive buffer is a list of chunks allocated with `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` (2^17 bytes) of capacity and filled by appending, never a contiguous buffer grown by doubling; a full chunk moves to the opposite endpoint's send buffer by ownership, without copying; the partly filled tail chunk moves only when the send buffer is empty and the downstream peer can take data now (the client-facing stream and connection windows both have credit for at least one byte), and it then becomes the send buffer's head and drains as credit allows; otherwise the receiver keeps appending to the tail (an implementation may instead copy the tail's bytes into the send buffer's last chunk when that chunk is also the send buffer's head and has room, a copy of at most one chunk, after which the tail chunk is released); the hand-off is checked on two events, when DATA is appended to the tail and when the send buffer drains or the downstream stream or connection window reopens; an emptied chunk is released, not kept; so at any time a stream holds at most two partly used chunks (the receiver's tail being filled and the send buffer's head being drained, together less than 256 KiB of capacity not counted by Σ) whatever DATA frame size the origin uses: small frames sent to a stalled client fill one tail until it is full and moves as a full chunk, instead of each pinning a chunk of its own; **small streamed bodies stay prompt** (server-sent events, small chunked responses): a small DATA frame either moves at once, because the send buffer is empty and the client can take it, or waits only while it could not be sent anyway (the client has no credit, or earlier bytes of the same stream are still queued ahead of it), and the drain or window-reopen event moves it as soon as it can be sent; `RST_STREAM`/`GOAWAY` mapped to stream errors; `http2_ping_keepalive`; `normalize_outbound_headers` (default true: lowercases outgoing h2 names and logs "Lowercased ... header as uppercase is not allowed with HTTP/2", `_http2.py:355-362`); `normalize_h1_headers` on h1→h2 (`_http2.py:340-352`), which applies the four steps of `h2:h2/utilities.py normalize_outbound_headers` as of h2 4.4.1: lowercase names, strip surrounding whitespace from names and values, drop the `CONNECTION_HEADERS` set (`connection`, `keep-alive`, `proxy-connection`, `transfer-encoding`, `upgrade`), and mark `authorization`/`proxy-authorization` and cookies shorter than 20 bytes as HPACK never-indexed; `validate_inbound_headers`; h2c upgrade and prior knowledge are refused by the default mitmdump set, which always loads the `disableh2c` addon (not an option). A Go embedder may omit that named addon to reach the h2c endpoint; HTTP integration owns separate negative-default and positive-omitted-addon tests, including buffered preface bytes and upgrade HTTP2-Settings decoding/ACK and the HTTP/1 request's stream-1 ownership; h1↔h2 interop (`py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http_version_interop.py`); `BufferedH2Connection` send-buffer semantics (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/_http_h2.py`).
2. Public `go:websocket/` state model gains `Fragmentizer`; relay lives at `go:internal/proxy/layers/websocket`. HTTP integration writes the 101 handoff against this unit's frozen interface, with negotiated extension validation and copied already-read bytes (`gows:conn.go:204-212`, WithBuffered). `Fragmentizer` follows `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/websocket.py:227-272`: when forwarded plaintext length equals the sum of original plaintext fragment lengths (unchanged or same-length edit), reuse those lengths including zero-length fragments; otherwise (length-changing edit or injected content without original fragments) use 4000-byte pieces and FIN on the last. For uncompressed messages exact original sizes are preserved. **Documented compression deviation**: gows DecodeFrame exposes plaintext only at FIN, so its original plaintext length list has one whole-message element; unchanged/same-length compressed messages are forwarded as one frame even if received fragmented, unlike upstream wsproto's per-frame inflation. Length-changing edits still use 4000-byte pieces. WebSocket relay supplies its compatibility row to the `docs/compat.md` owner (presentation and executable gates). Compression is terminated separately per peer with its own parameters/dictionaries and legal RSV1, not raw wire-image fidelity. Relay Ping/Pong/Close without auto replies, preserve absent/valid close code/reason; publish closed-by-client/code/reason/timestamp_end through snapshots. `websocket_start/message/end` are the upstream hooks; there is **no websocket_error hook**: abnormal closure sets flow error and still follows the terminal websocket_end lifecycle exactly once. One HTTPFlow owner receives both directions and cloned injections through the real Handler path, applies hooks, drop/kill/intercept, and consumes snapshots. Existing core `websocket` option and `dropped`/`injected` flags are reused.
3. UDP transport plus `go:internal/proxy/layers/udplayer`: admit `reverse:udp://` and `reverse:dtls://` at modeserver, create UDP listeners and UDP origin dialing, and set transport/address metadata via dispatch. One shared listener demultiplexes the fixed client/server 4-tuple to a distinctly named internal per-flow packet transport (not public `flow.UDPFlow`). It implements virtual `net.PacketConn` with packet-preserving ReadFrom/WriteTo, context cancellation and independent deadlines, bounded packet count/bytes per flow and listener, surfaced overflow/errors, and packet-preserving recorder/replay; never feed it through the byte-stream Peek/Read recorder or apply TCP half-close. Freeze numeric queue bounds at contract review and test them, not unbounded channels. Closing/expiring one virtual flow removes its demux entry and queued packets without closing the listener; tuple reuse creates a new flow, no migration. nextlayer gains UDP/DTLS kinds and transport branch, with raw `udp_hosts` selection using the existing regexp2 timeout/error rules (no duplicate regex engine). Raw relay uses public UDPFlow messages and `udp_start/message/end/error`, ignore bypass and cloned injection. Extend watchdog timeout selection from TCP/tcp_timeout (600 s default) to **UDP/20 s from the last datagram**, using its injected clock: disarm during hooks, rearm on return from the last-datagram baseline, and expire overdue flows; shutdown evicts all entries.
4. **DTLS** (added to scope by the user): `go:tlsparse` DTLS record and ClientHello parsing ported from `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/tls.py:94-160` (`dtls_handshake_record_contents`, `get_dtls_client_hello`, `dtls_parse_client_hello`; `starts_like_dtls_record` from `py:mitmproxy/net/tls.py:322`); the TLS layer's DTLS branch over the internal virtual packet transport uses pion v3.1.10's actual `dtls.Server(net.PacketConn, net.Addr, *dtls.Config)` and `dtls.Client(net.PacketConn, net.Addr, *dtls.Config)` constructors (no net.Conn overload). Shared `hookdata.TLS` preserves `Config *tls.Config` and adds `DTLSConfig *dtls.Config`, selected by `IsDTLS`; hookdata imports the pinned pion package. Validate nil/wrong-transport configuration before handshake. DTLS owns `addons/tlsconfig/dtls.go`, builds configuration under dispatch and honors hook overrides, mapping `client_certs`, `request_client_cert`, `ssl_verify_upstream_trusted_ca`/`ssl_verify_upstream_trusted_confdir`, `ssl_insecure`, available ciphers and key logging (reject unsupported TLS cipher selections explicitly rather than ignoring them). Adapt synchronized CertStore lookup to pion ClientHelloInfo, including no-SNI listener/origin SANs; leave Certificates empty with installed GetCertificate (see §1.4). Apply SupportedProtocols for ALPN and KeyLogWriter for SSLKEYLOGFILE. Preserve eager/lazy server-first ordering, `tls_clienthello`/start/established/failed hook ordering, and dispatch-published peer certificates/cipher/version/ALPN/timestamps; transport failure has no false established hook. ClientHello parsing ports upstream's ordered record concatenation and its vectors, not arbitrary out-of-order/fragmented handshake reassembly: explicitly test truncated/malformed, ordered fragments, reordered fragments and retransmissions, and document unsupported cases instead of claiming broad DTLS reassembly. DTLS ClientHello/head deadline is separate from idle expiry. `reverse:dtls://` shares UDP admission/transport; established tls_version is `DTLSv1.2`. **DTLS version rule** (the single rule for `tls_version_client_min`/`_max` and `tls_version_server_min`/`_max` under DTLS, also referenced by §10.4): the options keep their TLS choices `UNBOUNDED`, `SSL3`, `TLS1`, `TLS1_1`, `TLS1_2`, `TLS1_3` (`py:mitmproxy/net/tls.py:47-53`, `py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:145`; there is no DTLS value, and upstream passes the same values to its DTLS context, `net/tls.py:162-163`); pion negotiates DTLS 1.2 only (`pkg/protocol/version.go:33-37`), and DTLS 1.2 is derived from TLS 1.2 (RFC 6347 §1); a side's [min, max] window, with `UNBOUNDED` as an open end, is **accepted** when it contains `TLS1_2` and then negotiates DTLS 1.2; this covers the defaults (min `TLS1_2`, max `UNBOUNDED`, `net/tls.py:69-70`) and every min in {`UNBOUNDED`, `SSL3`, `TLS1`, `TLS1_1`, `TLS1_2`} combined with a max in {`UNBOUNDED`, `TLS1_2`, `TLS1_3`}; it is **rejected** when min is `TLS1_3` or max is `SSL3`, `TLS1` or `TLS1_1`. The check runs per DTLS connection at `tls_start_client`/`tls_start_server` (not in `configure`, because the same four options also govern TLS, where those windows are valid); a rejected window starts no handshake, fires `tls_failed_client`/`tls_failed_server`, and logs `DTLS in mitmproxy-go supports DTLS 1.2 only; tls_version_<side>_min=<value> and tls_version_<side>_max=<value> exclude it.` (`<side>` is `client` or `server`); DTLS supplies the version-rule compatibility row to the `docs/compat.md` owner (presentation and executable gates), because upstream's outcome for these values depends on the libssl build.
5. mitmdump `dumper` output for h2/WS/UDP flows as upstream (`py:mitmproxy/addons/dumper.py`).
6. Fuzz targets: h2 HEADERS assembly, WebSocket frame relay (seeds from RFC 6455 examples and gows's test vectors), DTLS ClientHello parser.

Acceptance criteria
- h2: `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http2.py` scenarios re-expressed as integration tests over `net.Pipe`-backed endpoints (header order, trailers, RST, GOAWAY, flow-control stall and release, 1xx) pass; a secure-web-proxy client offering `h2,http/1.1` negotiates `http/1.1`; with a Framer-level test client and a Framer-level test origin: (a) `validate_inbound_headers=false` and `normalize_outbound_headers=false` → the decoded header list including `X-Foo` is identical in both directions; (b) default options → an inbound `X-Foo` is answered with GOAWAY `PROTOCOL_ERROR` and the log contains `HTTP/2 protocol error: Received uppercase header name b'X-Foo'.` (upstream turns h2's validation error into a protocol error, `_http2.py:275-277`, and `protocol_error` puts the peer address in front of the message, `:319`); (c) `validate_inbound_headers=false` with `normalize_outbound_headers=true`, or a `requestheaders` addon that sets `X-Foo` → the origin receives `x-foo` and the log contains `Lowercased 'X-Foo' header as uppercase is not allowed with HTTP/2.` (`_http2.py:359`). Inbound names are never lowercased silently.
- A 64 MiB upload and a 64 MiB download concurrently on one h2 connection complete within 30 s; with a `requestheaders` intercept on one stream, the client keeps reading/framing and returning connection credit but withholds that stream's WINDOW_UPDATEs; the unrelated stream on the same connection receives its **full** response within 2 s. A separate stopped-TCP-reader scenario proves bounded queues, Send cancellation and released receipts, not unrelated-response completion; budget test on one h2 connection, with the Framer-level test origin advertising `MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS` ≥ 100 so that all 100 streams ride one upstream connection (the proxy advertises `MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS` 100, so a 101st stream would be refused by RFC 9113 §5.1.2 and a second connection has its own budget, so neither proves the bound), with `stream_large_bodies=1m`, a `content-length` of 64 MiB on each of the first 99 downloads and of 4 MiB on the 100th, so that all of them are streamed (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:631-649`; a buffered body would be the flow's content and is outside this budget, deliverable 1); the Framer-level test client keeps reading its socket throughout and returns connection-level window for everything it reads (it also advertises a connection window of at least 132 MiB, the 128 MiB budget plus the 100th stream's 4 MiB), advertises a stream `INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE` of 1 MiB (pinned so that what the proxy can still deliver on a stream after the client stops returning that stream's window is bounded, and the stalled state is the same on every run), and stalls a stream only by withholding that stream's `WINDOW_UPDATE`s, so the proxy→client hop never runs out of connection credit; the in-process test client and origin generate and discard DATA from fixed buffers, so their heap does not grow with the test; 99 download streams first read at full speed (the client returns their stream windows, so their proxy-side windows grow by the deliverable 1 trigger), and the client stalls each of them once it has received at least 8 MiB on that stream (it stops returning that stream's window); the 8 MiB threshold makes growth complete before the stall: a stream reaches 16 MiB after 0.5 + 1 + 2 + 4 = 7.5 MiB consumed, so by then every stream has passed each growth trigger, and if any headroom were left every trigger would have been granted in full and all 99 streams would be at 16 MiB, which needs 99 × 15 = 1,485 MiB of headroom, so the 28 MiB headroom is used up; while they are stalled the Σ-granted counter of the upstream-facing endpoint reaches 127 MiB (99 MiB reserved plus the 28 MiB headroom, which proves growth happened) and is ≤ 128 MiB at **every grant/release transition**, with synchronized snapshot/maximum and 50 ms samples as diagnostics only; the 100th stream, opened afterwards with its stream windows returned by the client, completes a 4 MiB download within 2 s on its reserved 1 MiB window; `/gc/heap/live:bytes` from `runtime/metrics`, measured in a dedicated process without parallel tests or unrelated retained fixtures, with **both endpoint owners kept alive** through both readings and the stalled/charged queues confirmed before the second GC; read after `runtime.GC()` at the idle baseline and again while the 99 streams are stalled, grows by at most 160 MiB: 128 MiB of DATA counted by Σ (whether still in the upstream-facing receive buffer or already in the client-facing send buffer), at most two partly used 128 KiB chunks per stream that Σ does not count (100 × 256 KiB = 25 MiB), and about 3.5 MiB for the two Framers' read and write buffers, the HPACK tables and the stream structs, which is 156.5 MiB with 3.5 MiB of slack; the budget test is run a second time with one change, the Framer-level origin sends its DATA in 1 KiB frames (the proxy re-frames to the client's `MAX_FRAME_SIZE` either way), and the same Σ assertions and the same 160 MiB heap bound hold, which checks the chunk hand-off of deliverable 1 under small frames; a response streamed by a `responseheaders` addon that sets `flow.response.stream = True` (`py:mitmproxy/http.py:247`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/__init__.py:427`), whose origin sends a 100-byte DATA frame every 500 ms, delivers each frame's bytes to the client within 100 ms of the origin sending it; throughput floor on ubuntu-26.04 with `tc qdisc add dev lo root netem delay 50ms` and `net.ipv4.tcp_rmem`/`tcp_wmem` maxima raised to 64 MiB: the netem test client advertises a 16 MiB stream window (`INITIAL_WINDOW_SIZE`) and a 16 MiB connection window, both through the proxy and on the direct path, because each hop through the proxy is capped at min(client window, 16 MiB)/RTT and a larger client window would make the floor unreachable; a single-stream 256 MiB h2 download through the proxy reaches at least 80% of the throughput of the same client→origin transfer without the proxy under identical settings.
- WS: on ubuntu-26.04, the pinned gows Autobahn container/config is run against `mitmdump --mode reverse:http://127.0.0.1:<echo-port>` and a ready echo origin. Assert the **expected nonzero complete case set and required groups**, not merely zero FAILED; reject FAILED, missing cases, and unexpected UNIMPLEMENTED. NON-STRICT/INFORMATIONAL are allowed; 6.x UTF-8 fail-fast cases are still run and only specifically justified deviations in `docs/compat.md` may be exempted, with code/reason and actual outcomes archived. Frame-aware peers verify exact uncompressed fragment sizes including empty fragments, same-length edits versus 4000-byte length-changing edits, and the stated compressed FIN-only boundary deviation. Ping reaches the origin and its Pong reaches the client, no unsolicited reply; absent/valid close code/reason, 101 plus a complete/partial first frame in one read, and differing per-side deflate parameters work. Through real Handler hooks, modification/drop/injection/kill/intercept and simultaneous directions are race-clean and terminal state appears in snapshots.
- UDP: map every `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_udp.py` case to a Go case or an explicit not-applicable reason; include real listener/dialer Handler paths, packet boundaries through recording/replay, cloned injection, hook edits/drop, ignore, errors and bounded overflow. Two tuples remain isolated; Close/expiry/shutdown remove entries, a reused tuple gets a new flow, and one virtual Close leaves the listener/other flows alive. Injected watchdog clock tests with real packet peers assert no expiry before 20 s, expiry at the boundary, reset by another datagram, hook disarm/rearm from last datagram and tuple eviction; no 20 s sleep or wall-clock proof. `udp_hosts` matching and regex timeout/failure behavior select raw UDP rather than DTLS.
- DTLS: `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_tls.py` DTLS ClientHello vectors ported; a pion `dtls.Client` with SNI `example.test` through `reverse:dtls://origin` to a pion `dtls.Server` origin completes both handshakes, the client sees a leaf for `example.test` signed by the CA, `tls_clienthello` carries the SNI, and 100 datagrams relay in order; a pion `dtls.Client` with an empty `ServerName` (pion then sends no SNI, `flight1handler.go:129-131`) completes the handshake through the same mode with its listener pinned to `127.0.0.1` (`reverse:dtls://origin@127.0.0.1:<port>`, the `@` listen suffix of `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:103-135`; a wildcard bind would make `client.sockname[0]` `0.0.0.0`) and receives a leaf whose SANs contain `127.0.0.1` (the listen address, `py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:619`) and the origin address (`:622-623`); with `ssl_insecure=false` and a self-signed origin the server handshake fails and `tls_failed_server` fires; version rule (table test over all 36 min/max pairs per side): accepted pairs negotiate DTLS 1.2, rejected pairs fire `tls_failed_*` with the logged text from deliverable 4, and the same pairs on a TLS connection are not rejected by this rule.
- Option table: `http2`, `http2_ping_keepalive`, `websocket`, `udp_hosts` are **already registered by core**; `normalize_outbound_headers` and `validate_inbound_headers` are already registered by proxyserver. Compare each upstream name/type/default/help once and test behavior; do not re-register. `disableh2c` is an always-loaded addon, never a `disable_h2c` option.
- HTTP integration: positive negotiated h2 and `http2=false` downgrade; mixed h1/h2 client/origin pairs under regular CONNECT and reverse HTTPS; secure explicit-proxy exception, preset/no-offer/no-origin-ALPN cases. Select endpoint factories from dispatch snapshots, never live concurrent ALPN fields. Default-addon h2c upgrade/prior-knowledge rejection and embedder-omitted-addon positive tests are separate; upgrade validates HTTP2-Settings and preserves request stream 1 and buffered preface bytes. Remove both HTTP/2 replay gates; replay saved h2 flows to h2 and h1 origins with preserved identity, hook/header normalization, connection failure, cancellation and streamed bodies; keep HTTP/3 rejection. Bound replay loading with flowio/har MaxEntries. h1 head/h2 preface/incomplete HEADERS and DTLS ClientHello trickle tests verify dedicated head deadlines and cleanup independently of idle timeout.

#### Executable gate recipe and testability

Deterministic protocol/clock/ownership tests run under `-race` on ubuntu-26.04, xcode-27 and windows-2025. Case mappings are exhaustive, not a representative subset; no required case may be skipped or stubbed. Performance deadlines below are explicit exceptions for isolated acceptance measurements, not unit-test wall-clock complexity proofs. Ordinary hang detectors are at least 10 s and dump goroutines/owner state on failure.

Presentation/gates adds a **required dedicated ubuntu-26.04 job** (not continue-on-error), separate from the existing generic proxy integration job. It checks/installs iproute2 and Docker, records versions and exact commands, and uses a dedicated network namespace/topology so netem does not affect unrelated tests. Explicit sudo installs 50 ms netem on the isolated loopback and sets TCP read/write maxima to 64 MiB; snapshot existing qdisc/sysctls before changes and install EXIT/INT/TERM restoration traps before setup. Serialize direct and proxy 256 MiB measurements under identical client/origin buffers, 16 MiB stream/connection windows and network settings; record bytes and measured monotonic durations for both. Restore even on test failure. The whole-transfer 80% floor cannot be changed to a steady-state interval after failure.

The same isolated job runs the 30 s transfer, 2 s unrelated-response, 160 MiB heap and 100 ms SSE gates in separate, nonparallel processes with explicit scenario/run deadlines and owner/credit/goroutine/network diagnostics. Heap archive includes baseline/stalled raw values, retained-owner and full-queue checks, maximum Σ and the 1 KiB rerun. These gates are requirements to measure, not proven performance claims; failures block landing and are not weakened automatically.

Pin Autobahn to `crossbario/autobahn-testsuite@sha256:519915fb568b04c9383f70a1c405ae3ff44ab9e35835b085239c258b6fac3074` (`gows:autobahn/run.sh:20`) and copy `gows:autobahn/config/fuzzingclient.json` from the consumed gows commit: case groups 1–7, 9, 10, 12, 13 and empty exclusions, changing only target URL/agent/output paths. Freeze the exact expected case IDs for that digest/config as a checked-in nonzero manifest before running the gate; no distro wstest assumption, floating image or report-generated self-validating manifest. Launch echo origin and mitmdump with readiness probes and teardown traps, use a bounded runner deadline, and assert report-case equality against that checked-in nonzero manifest with all required groups. Check outcomes as specified above; exemptions list individual executed 6.x cases and reasons, never broad omission. Archive reports, config/image digest, logs, command provenance, gate measurements and failure diagnostics; artifact upload runs on success and failure. Missing setup/privilege/image/report/cases is a failed required job, not skip-as-success.

### The parallel round: P4 and P6 together (revision 10)

**Outcome (revision 11)**: the round is closed. Every P4 and P6 unit and every follow-up is on main; the verified head is `6b098ca` (verifier report `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/reviews/verify-p46.md`: PASS, 0 blocking, 14 non-blocking NB-1 to NB-14), the post-round dependency commit `6161318` sits on top of it (gvisor `20261009-bd481cb1`, go-ldap v3.4.15, pion/transport v5.1.1, x/exp `20261007`, x/time v0.16.0; delta security review ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES with two low findings, L1 and L2). The deviations the round produced are ratified and folded into §3.4; the items the closeout funded form the next round, "The follow-up round after P4+P6", below P6. The text of this subsection and of P4 and P6 stays as written for the record.

**Order decision (user, 2026-10-07 15:35 JST)**: the gVisor-based packet sources are wanted earlier than the P4 → P5 → P6 order of revision 9. All of P6 (netstack, WireGuard mode, TUN mode, local mode on macOS, Windows and Linux with redirector acquisition) is brought forward and runs **in parallel with P4** (DNS, QUIC and HTTP/3) in the round that follows the P3.5 follow-up round; P5 (transparent and socks5) follows both. P6's engines depend on nothing in P4 or P5 (QUIC runs over the internal packet transport, netstack serves only the packet sources); the one cross-track dependency is the `modeserver` transfer, which waits for the P4 track's reverse-mode bootstrap, as the round rules below state. The one P5 artefact P6 reused, the `TransparentProxy` top layer, moves into the P6 track (P5 deliverable 2 now reads accordingly). Both tracks are built against main `d915d8f`, the head of the P3.5 round (P3 complete at `26bea03`, then the eight follow-up commits: present-unsent frame rejection, framer-error classification, bounded control queue, single local reset, GOAWAY flush grace, unsent-id release on `NO_ERROR` GOAWAY, reset-source labels, and the 64-candidate free-port helper with `GetFreeTCPPort`).

**What the two tracks share, and how the round keeps them apart**

- *One lead, one gate lock, two landing queues.* Every lane of both tracks gates under the same machine-wide lock before pushing; the lead pre-verifies each pushed commit read-only in a detached scratch worktree and lands it on main under the lock, one commit at a time, in the order the `PUSHED` reports arrive. **A lane may have only one unlanded pushed commit**: it waits for `LANDED` before pushing another, and the landing script refuses a branch that is more than one commit above main. **Rebasing happens before review, never after**: a lane rebases (`git r origin/main`, which re-signs and keeps author dates) before its gate and its push, so the hash the lead pre-verifies and the hash the Windows job ran on are the hash that lands; when main advances between a lane's push and its landing, the landing script refuses the non-fast-forward branch and the lane rebases, re-gates, re-pushes and obtains Windows evidence on the new hash (old-hash evidence is never reused for a new hash). The lead never rebases or rewrites a pushed candidate. The whole landing operation runs under the lock wrapper. Test-engineer lanes edit tests and test helpers only; production work, including every hardening helper, is assigned to executor lanes. The lock is an `flock(2)` on a file whose holder the kernel releases on exit, invoked directly (`bash <wrapper> <command>`), never through `bash -c`: the previous mkdir-and-remove lock was denied by a worker's harness when wrapped in an inline shell script, and race rows then had to be serialised by instruction. A lane holds the lock only for its gate and its stress rows, never while editing. Before spawning lanes the lead verifies that the `flock` executable the wrapper needs is present and that the wrapper exits immediately when its invocation fails (a missing binary is not a waiting lock); the wrapper's holder diagnostics read its `.holder` file.
- *Dependency-adding commits land first and alone.* P4 adds `codeberg.org/miekg/dns`, `github.com/quic-go/quic-go` and `github.com/quic-go/qpack`; P6 adds `gvisor.dev/gvisor`, `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard`, `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` and `google.golang.org/protobuf` (§4 pins). `go.mod` and `go.sum` are not union-mergeable, so **dependency-first applies once per newly introduced module, not once per unit**: the module's owner lands one commit that adds the module with `go mod tidy`, its `docs/dependencies.md` row, a compiling adapter file that imports the module (the §4 adapter rule) and that file's contract test, and nothing else; a dependency that nothing imports is pruned by `go mod tidy`, so no row lands without its importing file. The owners and files: `codeberg.org/miekg/dns` by DNS codec and resolver in `dns/miekg.go`; `github.com/quic-go/quic-go` by HTTP/3 integration in the spike test file named under P4 (the spike is the one bootstrap exception, see P4); `github.com/quic-go/qpack` by HTTP/3 engine in `internal/h3/qpack.go`; `gvisor.dev/gvisor` by netstack core in `internal/netstack/gvisor.go`; `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard` by WireGuard mode in `internal/wireguard/device.go` (its `tun` subpackage is imported by TUN mode later, no second row); `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` by Windows packet mode in `internal/local/winio_windows.go` (build-constrained to Windows; `winio_other.go` carries the implemented non-Windows refusal, returning the mode's `unavailable_reason` error, so every OS compiles and no body is a stub); `google.golang.org/protobuf` by local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode in the generated `internal/local/mitmproxy_ipc.pb.go` plus its decode test. Units that introduce no module (DNS layer and mode servers, QUIC sniff and raw layer, proxy core hardening, presentation and gates, TUN mode, Linux packet mode, packet-source modes and gates) start with their implemented contract or their first regression row; an API declaration never lands with unimplemented bodies. The lead lands a dependency commit before any other commit that imports the module, and a lane whose `go.sum` no longer rebases cleanly re-runs `git fetch origin && git r origin/main && go mod tidy` itself before pushing again. The lead never resolves a `go.mod` conflict by hand.
- *Exclusive package ownership across tracks.* The P4 track writes `dns/**`, `tlsparse/**` (QUIC Initial only), `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/**`, `internal/proxy/layers/quic/**`, `internal/h3/**`, `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/**` (HTTP/3 integration), `addons/dnsresolver/**`, `addons/stripdnshttpsrecords/**`, `addons/tlsconfig/**` (the `quic_start_client/server` handlers and their tests), `contentviews/dns*.go`, `flowio/flowjson` (the DNS branch), `addons/nextlayer/**` (DNS and QUIC selection), `addons/updatealtsvc/**` (the HTTP/3 listener hand-off), `addon/dispatch.go` and its tests (dispatch admission, a carried item), `internal/tools/dump/**` (default-addon registration) and `hack/network-gate.sh`/`hack/network-gate_test.sh` (the two HTTP/3 measurements). The P4 track also owns the **reverse-mode bootstrap** of `internal/proxy/layers/modes/modes.go`: on `d915d8f` its reverse-scheme switch rejects `dns`, `quic` and `http3` (`modes.go:42-43`) and `modeserver.New` admits only three modes (`modeserver.go:85-92`), so the DNS layer and mode servers unit lands, before any transfer, one commit that moves the reverse-scheme switch into a table in a new file `internal/proxy/layers/modes/reverse_schemes.go` (one entry per scheme: transport protocol, SNI rule, eager-connect rule, the layer kind the scheme selects) with the three P4 schemes present and the listener/factory extension point of `modeserver` (a per-scheme listener factory keyed by scheme and transport) frozen by its godoc and tests; the QUIC and HTTP/3 units later fill their table entries and factories in files they own (`reverse_schemes_quic.go`, `reverse_schemes_http3.go`, the HTTP/3 listener factory in `httplayer`) and never edit `modes.go` or the transferred `modeserver` files. The P6 track writes `internal/netstack/**`, `internal/wireguard/**`, `internal/tun/**`, `internal/local/**`, `internal/proxy/layers/modes/**` except that bootstrap (`TransparentProxy` and the packet-source top layers, in their own files), `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` after its explicit transfer, and `.github/workflows/packet-modes.yaml` (a new file). Neither track writes the other's packages; a need across the line is a `BLOCKED` to the lead, not an edit.
- *Serial transfers inside shared packages.* `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` is first written by the P4 track (DNS mode servers, the UDP listener side of `reverse:https`, dual-stack UDP listeners, the admission of `dns`, `quic` and `http3` reverse schemes and the per-scheme listener factory extension point) and becomes the P6 track's (`wireguard`, `tun` and `local` mode servers) **only by an explicit `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling from the lead**, issued after every P4 commit that touches `modeserver` or `modes.go` has landed (the DNS mode servers, the bootstrap table and factory, the dual-stack listeners); the transfer is never inferred from the first DNS commit. Until the ruling, P6's netstack, WireGuard, TUN, IPC and packet-mode units proceed on their own packages and their mode servers wait; if a later P4 protocol unit still needs a `modeserver` change after the ruling, the transfer is withheld or a shared-contract change is agreed and landed before either consumer. This is a real cross-track dependency (P6's mode servers wait for P4's bootstrap), and the plan does not claim otherwise. `internal/proxy/packettransport/**` and `internal/proxy/layer/**` are frozen: P4's QUIC layer and P6's netstack UDP forwarder consume `layer.PacketTransport` as it stands on `d915d8f`; a change there is a shared-contract commit that the lead lands alone before either consumer, with both tracks' consumers agreeing to the freeze first. `addon/hookdata/**` already carries the QUIC hook values and the DNS hooks; P4 adds nothing exported there without the same freeze. `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` stay append-only with one `## <package>` heading per package; a lane whose rebase conflicts only in those two files keeps both sides' sections (a union with no duplicate heading) in its own rebase and re-pushes, because the landing script refuses every non-fast-forward branch. Where two units of one track write the same file serially (`addons/nextlayer`: DNS first, then QUIC), only the second writer rebases, onto the first writer's landed commit; the first never rebases onto the second.
- *Two evidence workflows.* The existing `ci.yaml` (build, test, proxy integration, LDAP, gotip, lint, fuzz smoke, licences, difftest) and `network.yaml` (Autobahn and the isolated netem gates) are written by the P4 track's presentation and gates unit only, which also owns the `hack/network-gate.sh` and `hack/network-gate_test.sh` changes that run the two new HTTP/3 measurements sequentially beside the retained HTTP/2 and Autobahn rows (an absent or skipped required row fails the job). All evidence workflows keep workflow-and-ref-specific concurrency groups: a pull-request update may cancel the obsolete run of the same pull request, a push to main never cancels an active main run (`ci.yaml` and `network.yaml` already do this; `packet-modes.yaml` copies the same block rather than inventing a round-wide group); a pending run may be superseded, and round completion requires successful run ids for every required workflow on the final landed head. P6's privileged Linux rows (TUN under sudo, Linux local mode under passwordless sudo, WireGuard end-to-end) live in the new `packet-modes.yaml`, written by the P6 track's packet-source modes and gates unit, so the two tracks never edit one workflow file. CI runs only on push to main and on pull requests: every lane keeps a draft "Windows evidence (do not merge)" pull request open and **re-opens it after every landing**, because a fast-forward landing marks the open draft merged and a later push to the same branch then has no run.
- *Verification at the end of the round, not per track.* After both tracks have landed, one scoped security re-confirmation (`internal/h3`, `internal/proxy/layers/quic`, `internal/netstack`, `internal/local`: untrusted bytes from datagrams, redirector IPC and the virtual device) and one verifier pass (every regression row red on its parent and green on main, with the hardening evidence exceptions applied: preservation rows may be green on both revisions, and the load-only admission failure may retain honestly labelled historical nondeterministic parent evidence, neither reported as a reproduced deterministic parent failure; the stress and flake rows at the counts the gate recipe names; main CI and both evidence workflows green on the landed head) close the round; they run once, on the final immutable head after every criterion of both tracks is met, never on a partial track. The security pass runs on an unprefixed `security-reviewer` lane over the complete changed integration surface (including the QUIC TLS settings handlers and the packet-source glue) and reports by message; the lead persists the report verbatim. WireGuard and TUN may land while local-mode work is delayed, but the round stays incomplete until every required P6 criterion is met or the user explicitly changes the scope; experimental documentation waives neither the required Linux job nor the manual platform evidence.

**Carried into this round from the P3 and P3.5 reviews** (owner: the P4 track's proxy core hardening unit, listed in P4): local rejected-send cleanup versus endpoint failure so an intercepted `ErrorHook` can stay paused; the UDP relay's select race (`internal/proxy/layers/udplayer/udp.go`: the reader's `i/o timeout` after the interrupt deadline can be returned instead of `ctx.Err()`); dispatch admission that is not cancellation-aware; the pre-existing `context.WithoutCancel` sites in `internal/proxy` (`handler.go`, `handler_packet.go`, `pool.go`, `poolconn.go`); the failed-flight negative cache bound in the origin pool; the Windows `privfile` ACL hardening and its privileged-filesystem CI job (§10 item 12); the packet-transport loopback flake that reproduces only under a full-tree `-race` run (`TestTuplePackets/preserves_boundaries`: the recorded failure is the first-tuple admission wait in `acceptTestTuple`, the 10 s context at `transport_test.go:43-47` reported at `:64`, not the later `ReadFrom` deadline at `:70`; the hardening unit first distinguishes delayed admission from datagram loss with deterministic listener/peer readiness and positive delivery and admission signals, then repairs the admission fixture or the underlying cause without retries that erase failure evidence, keeps the read-boundary assertions separate, and retains a generous hang detector with a goroutine dump); the malformed-present framer `StreamError` path (a present identity whose frame the framer rejects still follows the stream-cancel path, `internal/h2/owner.go:219-221`), which this round retains as an explicitly unaddressed boundary, not certified repaired, and changes only by a separately agreed hardening item; the optional adoption of `freeport.GetFreeTCPPort` by `proxytest`; the client-facing 502 body built from an origin's GOAWAY debug data (at most 131,064 bytes of origin bytes, HTML-escaped to at most about 787 KB, control bytes kept: decide between a fixed client message with the debug kept in the log and the bounded escaped text, and record the choice in `docs/compat.md`); and the two redundant `len(controls) > 200` terms left in `internal/h2/owner.go` after the unified control-queue helper (keep their `connectionEvents` terms).

**Round pre-mortem (seven failure scenarios specific to the parallel round, each with its mitigation above)**: (1) DNS or QUIC codec tests pass while the CLI mode is still rejected — the reverse-mode bootstrap with its own executable `--mode` rows; (2) the QUIC hooks fire but no default addon supplies certificate and settings — the `addons/tlsconfig` ownership and rows; (3) `go mod tidy` prunes a dependency row, or two units disagree on their first commit — dependency-first once per module with a named importing adapter; (4) the malformed-DNS port is bent to a wrong expected output — the upstream-faithful criterion; (5) the loopback admission flake survives a read-deadline change — the admission-wait diagnosis and the two fresh full-tree runs; (6) a second push is bundled into a landing, or a hash is rewritten after its evidence — one unlanded commit per lane and rebase-before-review; (7) a privileged or manual platform row fails while the track is called complete — round completion only on the final head with every required row.

### P4 — DNS and QUIC/HTTP/3

**Pre-task (spike, 2 days)**: prove the internal per-flow packet transport's virtual `net.PacketConn` → `quic.Transport` hand-off with a sniffed two-datagram Initial on quic-go v0.63.0 before any P4 deliverable starts. The spike is the HTTP/3 integration unit's first commit and the one exception to the per-unit ownership rule: the unit temporarily owns one named file, `internal/proxy/layers/quic/transport_spike_test.go`, that builds a `quic.Transport` over a `layer.PacketTransport` fed from the packet-transport listener, replays two buffered Initial datagrams in order and completes a quic-go client handshake against an in-process quic-go server; the commit is the quic-go dependency commit (the `go.mod` row, `docs/dependencies.md` row and that test, which imports quic-go, nothing else) and ownership of the file passes to QUIC sniff and raw layer at the spike's landing. qpack is not carried by the spike: HTTP/3 engine lands it with `internal/h3/qpack.go`, which imports it. The spike lands before any P4 protocol consumer starts; DNS and hardening work proceeds independently of it.

#### Execution: units and ownership

This section is built against main `d915d8f`. What exists: `dns/` holds the flow-format-21 state shape (`Message`, `Question`, `ResourceRecord`, the constants with their display names and the `A`/`AAAA`/`TXT` constructors) and no wire codec; `flowio/flowjson` returns `dns flows are not supported yet` for a `*flow.DNSFlow`; `contentviews` has no `dns` view; `addon` declares `DNSRequestHook`/`DNSResponseHook`/`DNSErrorHook` and `QUICStartClientHook`/`QUICStartServerHook`, and `addon/hookdata` carries `QUICTLS`/`QUICTLSSettings`, so the hook surface needs no new exported type; `internal/proxy/modespec` parses every mode including `dns`, while `internal/proxy/modeserver` admits only regular, reverse and upstream (TCP listeners, and UDP listeners for `reverse:udp://`/`reverse:dtls://`); `addons/nextlayer` selects TLS/DTLS/UDP/TCP/HTTP and has no DNS or QUIC branch; `tlsparse` parses TLS and DTLS ClientHellos and no QUIC Initial; `internal/proxy/packettransport` demultiplexes a shared UDP socket into bounded `layer.PacketTransport` tuples, which is the transport the QUIC layer builds on; `addons/updatealtsvc` rewrites `Alt-Svc` to `ClientConn.Sockname.Port`; no HTTP/3 listener is implemented yet; `internal/h2`/`httplayer` carry the two-endpoint HTTP/2 engine and the adapter whose event model (`layer.EndpointDescriptor`, receipts, `Event` kinds) the HTTP/3 adapter reuses. Each missing integration has one owner below; a standalone codec test does not close an executable mode.

| Unit | Exclusive files / integration ownership | Tests and acceptance owned | Frozen output / waits | Landing order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| DNS codec and resolver | `dns/**` (codec glue, HTTPS/SVCB helpers, fuzz target), `addons/dnsresolver/**`, `addons/stripdnshttpsrecords/**`, `contentviews/dns*.go`, the DNS branch of `flowio/flowjson` | Every RR round-trip of `py:test/mitmproxy/test_dns.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/net/dns/test_*.py`; resolver system-server/hosts-file/interleave/error cases; `strip_dns_https_records` cases; `dns` view and flowjson DNS output against upstream fixtures | The codec API (`dns.Pack`/`Unpack` over the state shape, HTTPS record view) frozen by its first commit; the miekg dependency row | First in the track; codec commit before the layer unit's first test |

#### File ownership and serial transfers

- **DNS codec and resolver** is the only writer of `dns/**`; the state shape on main is not changed, the codec is added beside it. It also owns the three addon/view surfaces that depend on the codec (`dnsresolver`, `stripdnshttpsrecords`, the `dns` contentview) and the DNS branch of `flowio/flowjson`, whose other branches it does not touch.
- **DNS layer and mode servers** lands the reverse-mode bootstrap first (the table file `reverse_schemes.go`, the one edit of `modes.go` that replaces the switch by a table lookup, the `modeserver` admission of `dns`, `quic` and `http3` and the per-scheme listener factory, with `--mode reverse:dns://` executable and `reverse:quic://`/`reverse:http3://` admitted but answered by a frozen `not implemented yet` start error until their units land their entries), then writes `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` for the DNS listeners and the dual-stack UDP listeners of UDP-based modes. The package transfers to the P6 track's packet-source modes and gates unit only by the lead's `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling after the last P4 commit touching it has landed; after the ruling this unit never edits it again. It writes the DNS branch of `addons/nextlayer`; the QUIC branch of the same file belongs to the QUIC unit, and the two units land their `nextlayer` commits serially: DNS first, and only the QUIC unit rebases, onto the DNS unit's landed commit.
- **QUIC sniff and raw layer** writes `tlsparse/**` for the QUIC Initial parser only; the TLS and DTLS parsers on main stay as they are. It is the exclusive owner of the `addons/tlsconfig` QUIC handler additions and their tests: `quic_start_client/server` port the pinned upstream's configuration (leaf certificate, chain and key, ALPN, preservation of settings an earlier addon supplied, upstream verification and trusted-CA settings) and the layer converts the frozen `hookdata.QUICTLSSettings` to quic-go TLS configuration outside dispatch from dispatch-owned snapshots; the existing TLS and DTLS tlsconfig tests are preservation rows. No new exported hook type is needed. **HTTP/3 engine** writes `internal/h3/**`. **HTTP/3 integration** is the sole writer of `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/**` in this track apart from the two files the hardening unit names (`stream.go`, `driver.go`), which the two units touch serially (hardening first, because its change is small and its row must be red on `d915d8f`).
- **Proxy core hardening** writes the files its row enumerates and nothing else: not `modeserver`, not `layer`, not a packettransport production file, nothing in `dns/`, `tlsparse/`, `internal/h3/` or the P6 packages. Its `udplayer` change (return `ctx.Err()` when the context is already cancelled instead of the reader's deadline error) is one commit with a regression row that cancels while a reader is blocked and asserts the relay's error.
- **Presentation and gates** is the one writer of `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` in this track and of the two existing workflow files; the dependency rows of the units' first commits are the one exception (those commits land before any other commit of the unit, so no concurrent edit exists).

Deliverables
1. `go:dns/` codec glue over miekg v2 for the P0 state shape, HTTPS/SVCB record helpers (`py:mitmproxy/net/dns/https_records.py`; unblocks the `flowjson` DNS branch from P2), `dns` contentview (interactive; owned here, not by P7); DNS codec fuzz target. (DNS codec and resolver)
2. `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py`), `dns` mode servers (UDP and TCP :53), `reverse:dns://`, the UDP listener side of `reverse:https`, `dns_request/response/error` hooks (DNS layer and mode servers); `go:addons/dnsresolver` (`dns_name_servers`, `dns_use_hosts_file`; system servers from `/etc/resolv.conf` and the Windows IP helper API; hosts file parsing; A/AAAA interleave; `gaierror`-style errors); `go:addons/stripdnshttpsrecords` exactly as `py:mitmproxy/addons/strip_dns_https_records.py`: a `dns_response` hook that, when `strip_ech` (its own option, default true) is set, clears the `ech` SvcParam of every HTTPS answer, and, when `http3` is off, removes `h3` and `h3-*` ALPN ids (the ALPN list becomes absent when it ends up empty); it never removes records (DNS codec and resolver).
3. `go:tlsparse` QUIC ClientHello extraction from Initial datagrams (port of `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_client_hello_parser.py`: Initial secret derivation, header protection removal, CRYPTO frame reassembly across datagrams) for sniffing before deciding passthrough; QUIC Initial fuzz target. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
4. `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic`: `RawQuicLayer`/`QuicStreamLayer` for `reverse:quic://` (stream-level relay with `tcp_*`-style hooks as upstream), `quic_start_client/server` hooks and their default handlers in `addons/tlsconfig` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/tlsconfig.py:379-464`), which land before the interception handshake. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
5. `go:internal/h3` + layer: **own HTTP/3 framing** over `quic.Stream` with a frame parser ported from `qgo:http3/frames.go`: HEADERS/DATA/SETTINGS/GOAWAY, control stream, QPACK encoder/decoder streams (dynamic table capacity 0), unknown and GREASE stream and frame types ignored, h2-reserved frame types 0x2/0x6/0x8/0x9 treated as `H3_FRAME_UNEXPECTED` connection errors (`qgo:http3/frames.go:118-127`), closing a critical stream as `H3_CLOSED_CRITICAL_STREAM`, server push refused (`MAX_PUSH_ID` never raised, `CANCEL_PUSH` on receipt), H3 error codes mapped to stream resets both ways; `qpack` ordered header lists (HTTP/3 engine); client-facing HTTP/3 server and upstream HTTP/3 client; `http3` option; `reverse:http3://`; `tls_version` `QUICv1` in connection state; the HTTP/3 listener that P2's `update_alt_svc` points Alt-Svc at in reverse mode (upstream has no HTTP/3 in regular mode: `RegularMode.transport_protocol = TCP`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:186-191`) (HTTP/3 integration). The adapter reuses the HTTP/2 adapter's shape: one connection Receive owner per QUIC connection demultiplexing by stream id to one flow owner per exchange, `layer.EndpointDescriptor` publication under dispatch, original-byte receipts for DATA, bounded per-stream queues, and the same cancellation semantics; stream flow control is quic-go's, so the HTTP/2 budget text does not apply, and the bound the adapter owns is its per-stream receive queue (frozen at contract review, tested, never an unbounded channel).
6. mitmdump listeners for UDP-based modes (dual-stack). (DNS layer and mode servers)
7. Proxy core hardening: the carried items listed in the round preamble, one commit each with its regression row; the client-facing 502 body policy for GOAWAY debug data is decided by the lead at this unit's first report (the unit proposes; the default is a fixed client message `upstream closed the HTTP/2 connection` with the bounded, escaped debug text kept in the log line and the flow error) and recorded in `docs/compat.md` by presentation and gates. (Proxy core hardening)

Acceptance criteria
- DNS wire: round-trip of every RR type exercised in `py:test/mitmproxy/test_dns.py` and `py:test/mitmproxy/net/dns/test_*.py`; `strip_dns_https_records`: with `strip_ech=true` the `ech` parameter is cleared and all other SvcParams and records are kept; with `http3=false` an answer with ALPN `h2,h3,h3-29` keeps only `h2` and an answer with ALPN `h3` ends with no ALPN parameter; with `http3=true` ALPN is untouched. (DNS codec and resolver)
- `mitmdump --mode dns@5353` answers an `example.test A` query from an in-process miekg client, both over UDP and over TCP, using the resolver addon against an in-process miekg authoritative server (no external `dig` binary); a second query for the same name within the TTL is answered from the same upstream answer; `reverse:dns://<server>` forwards to that server; a pinned malformed vector whose header declares one question but whose question bytes are missing is sent over UDP and over TCP framing: parsing fails, the upstream invalid-message diagnostic is logged, only the offending connection or tuple is closed or evicted, no DNS flow is created and no `dns_error` hook fires (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:153-160`, `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/test_dns.py:50-67`), while other active UDP tuples stay up; separately, a valid request with no resolver answer and no upstream address fires `dns_error` exactly once and is answered `SERVFAIL` (`dns.py:103-110`, `test_regular_mode_no_hook`); a `FORMERR` answer is not implemented (it would be a compatibility deviation needing its own decision and `docs/compat.md` row). (DNS layer and mode servers)
- QUIC sniff: `tls_clienthello` fires with the correct SNI for a quic-go client's two-datagram Initial and every buffered datagram is replayed in order; `ignore_hosts` passthrough works for QUIC (origin completes its handshake directly); the Initial parser rejects a truncated CRYPTO frame and a wrong version without allocating beyond the datagram size. (QUIC sniff and raw layer)
- HTTP/3: with a frame-level qpack test client and a frame-level test origin, a request carrying duplicate lowercase names `x-foo: 1`, `x-bar: 2`, `x-foo: 3` arrives in the same order; with a GREASE frame and a GREASE unidirectional stream injected by the test client the connection stays open; `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http3.py`-derived scenarios (headers, trailers, stream reset, 1xx) pass; `reverse:https://origin` listens on TCP and UDP and rewrites `Alt-Svc` unless `keep_alt_svc_header`; a 64 MiB download through `reverse:http3://` completes within 30 s on the isolated Linux job, and an intercepted `requestheaders` on one stream leaves an unrelated stream's response complete within 2 s (the same two rows as HTTP/2, so the adapter proves the same isolation). (HTTP/3 integration; the two timed rows run on the isolated Linux job of `network.yaml`)
- Option table test: `dns_resolver`, `strip_dns_https_records` (`strip_ech`) and `http3` options equal upstream; `dnsresolver` and `stripdnshttpsrecords` are registered in upstream's `default_addons()` position and `TestAddonOrder` pins it. (Presentation and gates)
- Hardening rows: each carried item has a test that is red on `d915d8f` with the parent failure line recorded in the lane note (the paused `ErrorHook` row, the `udplayer` cancellation row, the dispatch-admission cancellation row, the `WithoutCancel` rows, the negative-cache bound row, the `privfile` ACL rows on Windows, the packet-transport admission row that distinguishes delayed admission from datagram loss, the 502-body row); the packet-transport repair is proved by two fresh full-tree `go test -race -count=1 ./...` runs in the gate plus `go test -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` on the named packettransport and UDP rows, with the exact commands recorded in the lane note (isolated successes are not full-tree-load evidence); the parent-red evidence for that row demonstrates the identified admission cause, or the lane note states that the historical load-only failure is retained as nondeterministic evidence rather than claiming a deterministic parent failure; the optional `GetFreeTCPPort` adoption and the removal of the two redundant terms are preservation work and are labelled as such. (Proxy core hardening)

#### Executable gate recipe and testability

Deterministic protocol/clock/ownership tests run under `-race` on ubuntu-26.04, xcode-27 and windows-2025. The per-commit gate is the P3 gate (format, lint with and without the difftest tag, build, vet on four targets, `go test -race -count=1 ./...`, the track's packages at `-count=5`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/proxy/...`), and before a unit's final push its own stress rows: the DNS layer at `-count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`, the QUIC hand-off and HTTP/3 scenario rows at `-count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, the three fuzz targets for 30 s each. The two timed HTTP/3 rows run in the isolated Linux job of `network.yaml`, added by presentation and gates beside the HTTP/2 rows, with the same archive of raw values. Every new regression row is red on `d915d8f` (or on the unit's parent commit) in a detached scratch overlay and green on the unit's tip; preservation rows may be green on both and are named as such; the packet-transport admission row follows the evidence rule of the hardening acceptance (a demonstrated cause, or the historical load-only failure retained and labelled as nondeterministic evidence).

| Criterion | Status against main | Required change / evidence | Owner / execution |
|---|---|---|---|
| DNS RR round-trips, HTTPS/SVCB, strip rules | State shape only, no codec | Codec over miekg with every upstream RR case; strip cases; fuzz target | DNS codec and resolver; all three OSes |
| `dns` view and flowjson DNS | Absent / rejected | View and JSON against upstream fixtures | DNS codec and resolver; all three OSes |
| Reverse-mode bootstrap, DNS mode servers, reverse:dns, TCP framing, resolver | modeserver admits three modes; `modes.go` rejects `dns`/`quic`/`http3` | Bootstrap table and factory rows; `--mode reverse:dns://` executable; in-process miekg client/server rows over UDP and TCP; malformed-vector close/log/no-hook row; SERVFAIL-with-one-error-hook row | DNS layer and mode servers; all three OSes |
| QUIC Initial sniff, ordered replay, passthrough, QUIC TLS settings | No parser, no hand-off, tlsconfig marks QUIC not applicable | Spike row, parser vectors, two-datagram replay, `ignore_hosts`, tlsconfig certificate/ALPN/CA/`ssl_insecure` rows | QUIC sniff and raw layer; all three OSes |
| HTTP/3 framing, QPACK, reserved/critical/push rules | Absent | Frame-level peers, exact error codes, GREASE tolerance, fuzz target | HTTP/3 engine; all three OSes (fuzz on the existing Linux job) |
| HTTP/3 scenarios, Alt-Svc, 64 MiB / 2 s | Absent | Scenario rows on all OSes; timed rows on the isolated Linux job | HTTP/3 integration; all three OSes, timed rows Linux (contributor: presentation and gates runs the job) |
| Carried hardening rows | Open items of P3/P3.5 | One red-on-parent row each (or an honestly labelled preservation row); two fresh full-tree race runs plus `-count=200` on the named packet and UDP rows, commands recorded | Proxy core hardening; all three OSes (Windows ACL rows on the Windows job) |
| Options, registration order, dumper | Needs ownership wording | Compared once; `TestAddonOrder`; upstream DNS/HTTP/3 dumper output | Presentation and gates; all three OSes |

### P5 — transparent and socks5 modes

### The transparent and socks5 round (revision 15)

**Context and approval boundary.** p51 starts at immutable main `9442ddaeef557f8e7027c17b724fb37e9c82fcdc`. Implement the four P5 deliverables plus the two explicitly bundled p50 follow-ups, not a new protocol or architecture round. Revision 14 is CLOSED under its recorded user disposition; its formal verifier FAIL, original count5 failure and isolated passes remain immutable history. This deliberate RALPLAN-DR draft requires separate architect/critic consensus and explicit user approval before execution. Keeping the P5 heading immediately above this round preserves phase navigation; the completed P6 section and revisions 11–14 remain historical records, not live file grants.

**Must have:** OS-original-destination lookup, transparent TCP admission on the landed destination-mode seam, SOCKS5 greeting/authentication/CONNECT with the existing hooks and proxyauth validators, CLI HTTP/HTTPS acceptance, Linux IPv4/IPv6 REDIRECT proof, macOS pf parsing and root ioctl proof, deterministic packet-boundary fixtures without losing separate real-wire coverage, and a bounded mechanism investigation of the direct h2 reference. **Must not have:** P7+, UDP transparent interception, SOCKS BIND/UDP ASSOCIATE support, a WinDivert port, native local-mode manual acceptance, workers executing A2/A3/N13, LD-17 trigger return, dependency/toolchain upgrades, broad layer/handler/netstack redesign, changed proxy/test/network retention/credit/size/ratio/deadline/watchdog/retry values, ad hoc reruns or package serialization, fabricated causal attribution or retrospective evidence edits. Preserve all 18 existing required app/context identities and the netem classifier/two-attempt/upload/restoration contract. Plan markers, unit numbers and NB/LD/R identifiers never enter source, tests, comments, scripts, workflows, manifests or commit messages.

#### Task flow, owners and dependencies

Six actionable units run on six authoring lanes: platform; mode integration; SOCKS/auth; executable/docs/gates; carried fixtures; direct diagnosis. One writer per package is binding; the table, not a broad glob inferred from a feature name, grants writes. Heavy tests and landings share the existing gate lock. The bottlenecks are the platform ABI/manual-root evidence, serial modeserver integration and hosted/gate queue, not author count.

| Unit / lane | Exclusive grant | Dependencies / first landable task |
|---|---|---|
| 1 / platform | New `internal/platform/**`, generated per-OS layout files and their provenance; no proxy packages | Starts immediately; implemented address/parser/OS contract first, then native lookup/self-check; no stub bodies |
| 2 / mode-integration | `internal/proxy/layers/modes/destination.go` and new transparent sibling tests; `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` only transparent/SOCKS admission, TCP acceptance, lookup wrapper and lifecycle tests; `internal/proxy/handler_source.go` and named source-destination tests only, and ONLY after the recorded serial transfer of package `internal/proxy` from unit A (see the graph: A lands the Config packet-dial seam and its conformance first, then the lead records the transfer; until then unit 2 prepares read-only); surgical TEST-ONLY grant on `addons/proxyserver/proxyserver_test.go:135–151` (base rows for transparent/socks5 configure assertions only), no `addons/proxyserver/proxyserver.go` change | Read-only prep immediately; transparent integration after 1; owns SOCKS modeserver import/admission after 3 lands. Each admission co-lands its matching configure-success/published-option assertions. `both.go`, candidate policy, packet sources and UDP paths preserved |
| 3 / socks-auth | New `internal/proxy/layers/socks5/**`; `addons/proxyauth/**` only SOCKS hook/validator reuse and tests | Starts immediately against existing layer/hookdata APIs; freeze real layer/auth contracts before 2 admits it; no mode-server edit |
