#### 1. Platform original-destination lookup

Create `internal/platform` with an implemented caller-context lookup contract `OriginalAddr(ctx context.Context, conn *net.TCPConn) (connection.Address, error)` and a platform-availability/refusal operation consumed at mode Start. The accepted socket stays caller-owned: use `SyscallConn.Control` to borrow its descriptor without `File()` duplication or closing it; never retain a raw descriptor beyond the callback or block on subprocess/I/O under addon dispatch. Preserve typed causes and validate returned address/family/length/port before publication. Linux uses `SO_ORIGINAL_DST` at IPv4 SOL_IP or IPv6 SOL_IPV6, selected from the accepted socket's local address after IPv4-mapped normalization, as `py:platform/linux.py:9–33`; do not try an inappropriate family syscall first. Decode sockaddr network byte order, exact sizes and mapped addresses; scoped raw calls get security review.

Darwin/FreeBSD use generated `pfioc_natlook` layouts and ioctl constants from a named pinned xnu/FreeBSD `pfvar.h` revision, checked in with generation command, header URL/revision and size/offset checks. The platform owner consults official headers before implementation; no guessed cross-OS layout, runtime compiler or dependency is introduced. Open `/dev/pf` with close-on-exec and close owned resources exactly once. On the first usable redirected connection per process/OS resolver, compare the ioctl tuple with bounded `sudo -n /sbin/pfctl -s state` output for the same peer; only an exact matching tuple validates the ioctl path. A mismatch or ioctl/layout refusal disables the unvalidated ioctl path and uses the text lookup; an absent/failed text match must not be presented as a passed self-check or a destination. Synchronize concurrent first-use checks without holding dispatch; cancelled waiters return promptly and bounded work joins. Use a 5-second subprocess deadline and 4 MiB output bound, fail explicitly on truncation/nonzero/malformed state; those are lookup-resource bounds, not changed proxy/network acceptance deadlines. Preserve upstream privilege text `Insufficient privileges to access pfctl. See https://mitmproxy.org/docs/latest/howto-transparent/#macos for details.` and `Could not resolve original destination.` where applicable. `pf01` yields `192.168.1.111:40000 → 5.5.5.5:80`, mapped IPv4 gives the same; its established IPv6 row yields `("2a03:2880:f21f:c5:face:b00c::167", 443)`. `pf02` covers FreeBSD parentheses. FIN_WAIT, wrong client/port, malformed/truncated/oversized output and substring-only matches must not produce a different flow's destination.

OpenBSD returns accepted getsockname/LocalAddr, matching `py:platform/openbsd.py`. Windows remains refused: pinned `windows.py:467–470` actually logs `Transparent mode on Windows is unsupported, flaky, and deprecated. Consider using local redirect mode or WireGuard mode instead.` while implementing legacy WinDivert; use that exact warning as the Go Start refusal text, and document that refusal is this port's deliberate limitation, NOT an upstream refusal behavior. Other unsupported OSes get an explicit unsupported-platform failure. No WinDivert acquisition or privileged helper daemon enters this unit.

Acceptance: portable tests `TestOriginalDestinationAddress`, `TestPFStateLookup`, `TestOriginalDestinationFailures`, `TestOriginalDestinationPFSelfCheck` cover family decoding, both fixtures, refusal/error/cancel/bounds, self-check match/mismatch/concurrency and exactly-once close. Native Linux calls are certified by unit 2 REDIRECT v4/v6; macOS actual ioctl/self-check is `TestOriginalDestinationPFIntegration` under `integration`, skipping only when effective uid !=0, with the root run mandatory separately. Run portable rows `-race -count=20 -cpu=1,2,8` (60 per selected parent/child), default+Windows+FreeBSD builds/vet, and parser inventory on ordinary xcode-27. New feature certification is not fabricated red-on-parent from a missing package/API. Parent-compatible pf parsing oracle vectors are preserved separately. Every raw-platform/root candidate receives the scoped security gate before landing.

#### 2. Transparent server and both new mode admissions

There is no Go `TransparentProxy` identifier at the base. In `modes/destination.go:init`, add exactly `layer.Register("transparent", newDestinationMode)` beside the existing wireguard/tun/local registrations; its `destinationMode.Run` already selects transparent protocol layers from `c.Data.Server.Address` and pre-connects TCP when eager. Preserve those existing modes and their error semantics. Do not create a second protocol chooser or rename all historical P6 references.

`modeserver.New` admits `modespec.TransparentMode` with kind `"transparent"`; `Start` checks platform availability before creating listeners, then uses existing TCP listen/accept/Stop mechanics. The mode-integration owner performs `platform.OriginalAddr` on each accepted raw `*net.TCPConn` before Handler/recording wraps it, outside dispatch; lookup failure closes only that client, releases its limiter slot and logs the original cause without dialing the proxy listener. An immutable wrapper preserves original LocalAddr/RemoteAddr/CloseWrite and exposes `GetExtraInfo("remote_endpoint")` as the recovered destination. Extend `packetSourceDestination` in `handler_source.go` to recognize TransparentMode and REQUIRE that captured metadata: unlike packet-source fallback, transparent may never treat the redirected listener's LocalAddr as an original destination. `Handler.Handle:113–146` then constructs `connection.NewServer(destination)` before hooks; no Handler body change or public Config expansion is necessary. Missing/invalid captured metadata fails closed. Keep `client.sockname` as the actual accepted socket address. Test that `c.Data.Server.Address` is the recovered original tuple before eager `Pool.Open`, for both eager/lazy and TLS/HTTP/TCP selection.

After unit 3 lands, the same owner admits `modespec.Socks5Mode` kind `"socks5"` and blank-imports its registering package in modeserver, preserving the default 1080 and existing mode parsing. No modespec grammar rewrite. New admissions are separate landable candidates if necessary; not an accepted listener with a missing registered layer. Existing TCP-only Stop does not join live accepted clients while dispatch is held; cancel Start context still closes clients. Duplicate addresses, setup failure, runtime mode change and limiter release retain existing contracts. Production Both candidate/error behavior, including the landed Windows candidate-path TCP retries, stays unchanged; the older p50 fail-fast draft sentence is not reintroduced.

Acceptance identities: `TestTransparentModeAdmission`, `TestTransparentDestinationHandoff`, `TestTransparentModeLifecycle`, `TestSocks5ModeAdmission`, `TestTransparentWindowsRefusal`. Inject lookup outcomes only at the OS boundary, not protocol-service mocks. Preserve wireguard/tun/local destination and regular/reverse/upstream start/stop tests. Parent-compatible admission test on 9442dda must fail assertion because New refuses the mode, not compilation. Focused mode tests run `-race -count=20 -cpu=1,2,8` with zero selected skip/fail.

**Admission-dependent configure assertions co-land:** within the surgical `addons/proxyserver/proxyserver_test.go:135–151` grant, update only the admitted transparent/socks5 row in the existing `TestUnsupportedModes` group to assert `Options.Update` succeeds with `server=false` and publishes the requested mode option. Keep rejection/rollback assertions for any mode not yet admitted until its own admission candidate; do not land a later test-fixup. Preserve `TestOptions` invalid-mode/rollback behavior unchanged and run both groups as focused preservation. Windows transparent configuration with `server=false` succeeds; its separate Start/refusal assertion remains mandatory. No `addons/proxyserver/proxyserver.go` change: Configure delegates at :167 to `configuredInstances`, and :283–287 continues to surface `modeserver.New` admission unchanged.

**Linux real acceptance, required, no skip-as-pass:** `TestTransparentRedirectAcceptance` (`integration && transparentprivileged && linux`) runs the prebuilt the real mitmdump executable with `--mode transparent`, a real origin and local curl client in isolated namespaces. Create namespace-specific IPv4 `iptables -t nat` and IPv6 `ip6tables -t nat` OUTPUT REDIRECT rules scoped to the origin tuple AND the test client identity, exempting the proxy's origin dial to prevent recursion. Record exact installed rules/counters, origin/listener/client addresses, curl exits, flow state and request/response hook counts. Both v4/v6 flows must report `server_conn.address` equal to the pre-NAT origin, not listener port; HTTP and HTTPS interception use the test CA, not `-k`. The separate transparentprivileged build tag excludes this root-only row from ordinary nonroot integration count5 without adding a skip. Missing IPv6/NAT/root is failure of this required row. Snapshot/install cleanup traps before mutations; restore/delete only owned rules/namespaces and join all processes on success/failure. Unit 4 appends this row to the existing `privileged packet modes (Linux)` job without changing its identity or removing current packet-source rows.

**macOS real acceptance:** unit 1's `-tags integration` ioctl test runs manually as root on the plan author's machine and records OS/header revision, exact command, ioctl+pfctl tuples, self-check decision and teardown in the PR/runbook receipt. The nonroot skip is only a portability result, not closure; root permission failure/missing state under the prepared test setup blocks acceptance. This is transparent PF evidence, not the excluded native local-mode runbook action.

#### 3. SOCKS5 layer and RFC 1929 authentication

Register kind `"socks5"` from new `internal/proxy/layers/socks5`. Reuse `layer.Context`, Recorder, `Pool.Open`, `Hooks.Fire`/`FireFunc`, Do and existing `addon.Socks5AuthHook{Data:*hookdata.Socks5Auth}`; hookdata already has Client/Username/Password/Valid, so add no exported hook type. Parse greeting, optional RFC 1929 credentials and CONNECT incrementally with exact bounded reads; preserve trickled/coalesced bytes and hand off application/TLS bytes exactly once without replaying SOCKS handshake bytes. Stop initial handshake recording before consuming control bytes, or equivalently prove a bounded discard/recorder handover with existing APIs; no core Recorder redesign. Buffers are bounded by one-byte method/name lengths and fixed address/port fields, not an unbounded grow-until-EOF slice. Handshake disconnect/cancellation joins all work; keep the existing watchdog/head bounds, no new timeout option.

Mirror pinned `modes.py:167–303`, not an assumed generic SOCKS library: proxyauth configured selects method 0x02, otherwise 0x00; missing selected method uses upstream's exact refusal bytes/log text; CONNECT accepts ATYP 1/3/4 with network-order port and upstream domain/credential decoding; unsupported request/version/reserved/cmd/ATYP paths use upstream bytes/text. BIND and UDP ASSOCIATE reply command-not-supported (0x07); unknown ATYP gives 0x08, eager connection failure 0x04, success uses the upstream zero BND tuple. Upstream sends a ten-byte 0x05/0xff refusal rather than only a two-byte choice, and its auth parser does not explicitly validate the RFC 1929 version or reject zero username/password lengths: port the pinned behavior and test these corners, document protocol discrepancies rather than silently claiming stricter RFC compliance. No-auth and auth coalescing, one-byte trickle and premature close all have explicit rows. Eager establishes destination before success; lazy preserves upstream success-before-dial behavior. Publish server destination under Do, obtain any eager pooled transport outside dispatch, and start `layer.Next` only after success/handover; protocol sniffing never consumes SOCKS bytes.

Add `ProxyAuth.Socks5Auth(ctx, data)` using the same configured single-user/any/htpasswd/LDAP validators. Accepted credentials mark Valid and populate the same weak-client authenticated cache as CONNECT, so subsequent HTTP/HTTPS requests over SOCKS are not challenged again and get upstream proxyauth metadata. Capture immutable LDAP config/credential inputs before `addon.Concurrent`; LDAP network I/O remains outside dispatch and cancellation/error behavior remains bounded. Never log username/password/raw auth frame in new diagnostics. For premature closure during credential subnegotiation, log only the stage and byte count rather than upstream's raw buffer repr; document that intentional credential-redaction difference and submit it with the scoped auth review. Hook validity is read via a bounded dispatch snapshot/Do; failure sends `01 01`, success `01 00`. Existing HTTP-only auth paths and weak-key cleanup remain preservation. Reuse the real OpenLDAP integration service for LDAP SOCKS acceptance, not a mock server.

Acceptance: map ALL seven upstream SOCKS functions (`test_socks5_success`, `test_socks5_trickle`, `test_socks5_err`, `test_socks5_auth_success`, `test_socks5_auth_fail`, `test_socks5_eager_err`, `test_socks5_premature_close`) and every parameter expansion, plus a separate Go no-auth method-selection group. Record exact Python-function→Go-case mapping in the lane handoff. New identities `TestSOCKS5Handshake`, `TestSOCKS5Authentication`, `TestSOCKS5Connect`, `TestSOCKS5Handover`, `TestSOCKS5Cancellation`; addon identities `TestSocks5Auth`, `TestSocks5AuthenticatedRequests`, `TestSocks5LDAP` (integration). Protocol tests use real net.Pipe/socket peers, t.Context, map tables/go-cmp and goleak; all portable rows `-race -count=20 -cpu=1,2,8`. Parent-compatible hook-dispatch row asserts valid=false on the base and true after the new handler; absent method compile errors are not red. `FuzzSOCKS5Handshake` parses bounded byte input with upstream seeds; 30-second focused fuzz gate and existing CI smoke discovery are mandatory. Every auth/layer candidate gets scoped security review before landing.

**Privileged-job budget is a pre-landing/pre-freeze gate owned by unit 4.** `.github/workflows/packet-modes.yaml:24` currently limits the ENTIRE `privileged packet modes (Linux)` job to 20 minutes; a per-command 20m outer/15m test watchdog is not an allocation within that job. Before landing its additive workflow candidate, record in this plan a receipt-backed budget using the LAST THREE GREEN runs of that same job: run ID/head/checkout, setup/tool installation, existing user builds/inventory, all five unchanged packet scenarios, teardown and always-upload durations, with raw receipt paths/digests. If the recorded projection does not fit, an orchestration-only `timeout-minutes` increase for THIS existing job is allowed ONLY after the full budget and proposed new total/reserve are recorded in the plan; preserve its context identity and every test count/cpu/watchdog/protocol/netem/acceptance value. #### A. Carried untouched-package fixture determinism

Preserve `.omc/artifacts/p50-session/reviews/verify-p50.md` Gaps 1–3 and the triage's chronology. The failed invocation was `go test -tags integration -count=5 -timeout=60m -json ./internal/proxy/...`, not an explicit race/cpu campaign; gopls/vet were not concurrently executing under the held gate lock. QUIC reached the receiver's idle cause instead of application 42/closed, not its publication-gate timeout; DNS waited at request-hook arrival during the first 1024-ID fill, not at 1025th rejection. Original fail + isolated 15/15 are historical evidence, not reproducible-root-cause proof.

First land bounded test-only stage/input diagnostics: QUIC source publication gate, target local-close, ACTUAL packet Write completion/result and receiver-close; local qlog alone precedes actual Write and does not prove receipt. DNS current transaction ID, write/input/tuple/owner/hook stage, original cause and done/finished state. On failure retain goroutines and the bounded event history, with no per-packet log flood, production hooks, sleeps/retries or changed timers. Then introduce a reusable test-only queued packet-preserving pair in `internal/proxy/packettest`, via existing layer.PacketTransport/net.PacketConn recorder interfaces. It must clone borrowed input, preserve FIFO and zero-length/truncated datagram semantics, provide fixed local/remote address identity, bounded queue/bytes, context/deadline wakeups and exactly-once Close/join. Test its cancellation/close/overflow and do not let queue acceptance synthesize a successful protocol result.

Use queued pairs in `TestDNSRetainedTransactionLimit/error:_UDP_unanswered_retained_bound` through existing ClientPackets/ServerPackets. Keep 1024 distinct retained IDs, same-ID at-bound flow reuse, 1025th fail-closed diagnostic/no-hook and finalization assertions byte/behavior-equivalent. Retain separate real-UDP forwarding/retransmission tests, including `TestDNSRetransmitBeforeResponse`; do not replace every UDP fixture. For `TestQUICWireCloseBeforeContextPublication/client_application_close`, keep real quic-go endpoints, source publication block and exact opposite-receiver application code/reason; route the boundary fixture's packet path through queued controlled delivery using a test listener/packet-connection factory in wire_test.go. Preserve server-close sibling and separate existing wire-session production-mode coverage (`TestQUICWireStreams`, `TestQUICWirePassthrough`, `TestQUICWireReset`). **Single acquisition seam, explicitly granted by R51-6:** add exactly `PacketDialer func(ctx, lifetime context.Context, server *connection.Server) (layer.PacketTransport, error)` to `proxy.Config` in `internal/proxy/handler.go`, mirroring Config.Dialer. Carry it through a private Handler field into BOTH packetServers constructors (`handler.go:143` for Handle and `handler_packet.go:77` for HandlePackets), then call it at `handler_packet.go:182` instead of the hardcoded `dialPacketServer(openCtx, p.ctx, snapshot, net.Dialer{})`. NewHandler defaults nil to a wrapper calling that SAME existing dialPacketServer with the same open context, Handler lifetime, private server snapshot and `net.Dialer{}`; nil therefore preserves today's UDP socket/buffer/source-address/cancellation behavior byte-for-byte, with no changed policy or new default worker. The queued fixture supplies this Config field to return the actual queued origin, uses a queued client PacketTransport through Handler.HandlePackets and retains the test-only RawQuicLayer config/publication wrapper, without replacing `layer.Context.OpenPackets`. Acquisition remains outside dispatch: `packetServers.open` fires server_connect and snapshots metadata, calls the injected acquisition with open and lifetime contexts, applies its existing canceled-open check, fires server_connected with actual endpoint metadata, appends THAT queued transport to its existing entries at :197–210, and returns its activity wrapper. Handler then closes the registered transport, joins pending opens and emits disconnect through the existing :213–229 ownership path when its client/layer ends; the queued transport's Close joins its own workers. The injection honors open cancellation and the distinct Handler lifetime; it must not bind a successful origin to the short-lived open context. **Queued-origin ownership conformance stays mandatory:** add success, cancellation, rejected-open and early-close subtests under the existing `TestQUICWireCloseBeforeContextPublication` parent, proving actual queued-origin registration through observed lifecycle/close behavior, existing hook order/metadata and exactly-once disconnect according to each existing path, and that no origin or worker outlives Handler. Include nil/default real-wire preservation and both Handler entry-point propagation; fixture-manager cleanup must not mask a leaked Handler-owned origin. Apart from adding/defaulting/propagating/calling this ONE field in the two named files, no other production edit in internal/proxy is permitted: no `layer.Context.OpenPackets` contract change, registry/hook/lifecycle rewrite, parallel registry, exported modeserver bind hook or ListenerFactory API. If the seam cannot preserve that existing contract, report the blocker rather than widening the grant. The test still runs RawQuicLayer/Handler; it never injects a terminal error as a substitute for a received CONNECTION_CLOSE. No production transport/library patch or origin-keepalive/watchdog change is granted.

Acceptance: both exact carried children and their parents complete at `-race -count=20 -cpu=1,2,8` (60 child passes each, zero skip/fail); `TestQueuedPacketTransport` and real-wire preservation rows pass the same profile; whole proxy integration `-tags integration -count=5` green without rerun. Keep the triage classification moderate/unproven until an actual cause is demonstrated: this is deterministic boundary-fixture hardening, not a claimed repair of an unrecorded packet-loss event or latent production defect. The candidate introducing Config.PacketDialer requires scoped grok security review before landing; test-only follow-ups remain exempt. Any other proposed production change is outside this grant.

#### B. Direct-reference stall: mechanism before remediation

The retained campaign sidecars establish the same validated stop `39,059,456 = 37.25 MiB`, actual partial-DATA `39,156,143`, wire/last payload offset `39,289,999`, stream 3 and live client/origin readers with no recorded failure/context cause except the measurement deadline. Campaign 1 has a two-attempt residual on ref17; campaign 2 refs11/13 have first AND retry stalls; campaign 3 refs14/17 first attempts stall and retries pass. Campaign 3 elapsed is `30,000,689,475`/`30,001,152,018` ns; passing retries finish full 268,435,456 payload bytes. These are direct client→origin measurements, not proxy throughput findings. Origin SEND connection and stream credit remain 14,548,992 (or 14,680,064) at the stall; client credit fields are its independent outgoing/request-side budget, not proof of received DATA credit. Thus a simple origin-credit-zero explanation is contradicted by the snapshots, but blocked credit-update writes, TCP/netem queues or a stalled frame reader remain candidates. The fixed offset alone proves none of them.

Read/audit all first/retry originals before authoring. Use the instrumentation-first ordering chosen in decision (d): establish terminal-state discrimination and local conformance first; add a deterministic local wait-cycle sensitivity before the single hosted probe when those receipts support a particular cycle, rather than assume it from the fixed byte count. This does not authorize an extra hosted dispatch. Add only bounded test-peer stage state needed to discriminate: current reader stage (frame header/body vs control write), current/queued frame-write kind+stream and requested/completed byte count, write-lock waiter/owner state, consumed/advertised/observed window updates, response-worker/body offset, first typed cause, plus once-only terminal goroutine stacks. Snapshot peer locks in the existing client→origin order, never call a locking accessor while holding its lock. Freeze measurement elapsed/byte state first, then collect diagnostic-only native socket/TCP_INFO and `ss -tinm`/`tc -s -j qdisc show dev lo` BEFORE cleanup while the namespace/sockets still exist. Unit 4 owns the narrow harness callback/namespace-scoped terminal capture; no periodic polling, measured-interval logging/clock loop or response retention is introduced. Tests certify snapshot-before-close, completed partial counts, no recursive-lock deadlock and non-direct lifecycle preservation. Rich output is separate sidecar/diagnostic files under existing NETWORK_GATE_OUTPUT, never new t.Log/NETEM records or classifier input. Capture failure is an explicit incomplete experiment, not a green diagnosis.

**Exact experiment on the existing netem job:** run its unchanged selected `TestHTTP2LoadNetem`, non-race, 256 MiB and matched 16 MiB windows, 50ms netem, 64 MiB TCP maxima, 30s measured wait, 5m peer context and existing 600s attempt. The workflow's existing `bash hack/network-gate.sh --netem-first`, first artifact upload, and eligible `--netem-retry` remain the carrier; diagnostic capture is armed for EACH attempt with a pre-cleanup terminal callback. Retain inventory, same-head checkout, first/retry sidecars, separate peer-stage/native/goroutine artifacts, qdisc/drop/backlog counters and restoration/upload/producer exits. The diagnostic patch is an explicitly identified probe candidate/head, never evidence that uninstrumented final main ran that patch. One diagnostic frozen-head dispatch is the bounded investigation campaign; do not run until a failure appears, induce a hosted failure or add a third attempt. A pass with no discriminating stall leaves the mechanism unproved.

Discriminating receipt: (a) credit exhaustion needs the blocked sender's ACTUAL matching stream/connection credit <=0 and recorded missing/blocked WINDOW_UPDATE path, not the 37.25 MiB label; (b) network queue/backpressure needs blocked native socket write plus TCP send/receive queue/counter and qdisc/drop/backlog state consistent with the stalled half-frame; (c) reader/control-write stall needs the live reader's stack/stage and blocked writer ownership, with pending input/queued bytes and lock state showing why it cannot drain. If one terminal snapshot cannot prove initiation, perform a deterministic local real-peer gated sensitivity that reproduces the hypothesized wait cycle and demonstrates removal on the candidate, without changing acceptance values. The diagnostic report lists alternatives ruled out and evidence limits, not an inferred cause solely from a matching byte count.

A proved TEST-PEER mechanism gets the smallest test-peer fix plus discriminating parent-compatible regression and preservation of every non-direct peer/load test. A proved PRODUCT mechanism is proposed with exact files/ownership, reviewed by the lead for the narrow grant, then implemented and scoped-security-reviewed before landing; no h2 engine/credit-window edit is pre-authorized. Changing a credit window, time budget, body size, netem queue limit, retry or ratio to bypass the stop is prohibited. If proof fails, deliver the diagnosis/observation and merge NO B source/test/workflow/harness change: experimental diagnostics stay on the separately identified probe branch with its diff and receipts archived, never on final main. This is the fixed no-change disposition, not permission to land instrumentation as a repair. Only a proved repair may bring its necessary bounded diagnostics/tests through reviewed candidates. NB-38 remains open; green retries do not close it. Product change triggers a final 20 distinct-ref same-SHA Network campaign; no product change uses standard Network gates only, not another unrelated 20-run campaign.

#### Executable focused command matrix

Commands below are for execution after approval, not planning. Run at repository root (no cd). Use `set -euo pipefail`; on this host add `/opt/homebrew/bin` to PATH after verifying `timeout`. Setup:

```bash
R=/Users/zchee/go/src/github.com/zchee/mitmproxy-go
GATE="$R/.omc/artifacts/p49-session/with-gate-lock.sh"
OUT="$R/.omc/artifacts/p51-session/evidence/focused/$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
mkdir -p "$OUT"
export NETWORK_GATE_OUTPUT="$OUT/direct-output"
export MITMPROXY_GO_HTTP2_LOAD=1
```

Each row's outer watchdog starts INSIDE `bash "$GATE" timeout ...`, after lock acquisition. Every command gets a distinct complete stdout/stderr log and receipt with expanded command, immutable SHA, host/toolchain, producing-command date start/end, exit and per-parent/descendant run/pass/fail/skip tallies plus SHA256SUMS. Preserve `>|` overwrite syntax. The table's `\|` escapes are Markdown only: executable regex alternatives use plain `|`. Require exact selected-name inventory equality before runs; empty/missing/unexpected selection fails. Native-only test inventories are audited on their native host, not invented from cross-builds. A timeout, skip in an acceptance row, truncated tally or unjoined worker is FAILED/INCOMPLETE. Only the expressly nonroot macOS ioctl portability invocation may report its expected privilege skip; its root acceptance is separately mandatory.

**Privileged/manual command carrier (native hosts only):** build before root execution; do not run a privileged `go test` that downloads/compiles arbitrary module hooks as root. Unit 4's existing Linux packet job builds mitmdump and the integration test binary as the runner user, then invokes the binary under bounded sudo in the prepared isolated topology. The same selected test owns rule/namespace setup and teardown; no whole-machine firewall flush. Exact commands:

```bash
bash "$GATE" timeout --signal=QUIT --kill-after=15s 15m go build -o "$OUT/mitmdump" ./cmd/mitmdump
bash "$GATE" timeout --signal=QUIT --kill-after=15s 15m go test -tags integration,transparentprivileged -c -o "$OUT/modeserver-integration.test" ./internal/proxy/modeserver
```

The test receives the built mitmdump path through its named environment input; `/usr/bin/env` sets only that one non-secret value and does not print the environment.

```bash
bash "$GATE" timeout --signal=QUIT --kill-after=15s 20m sudo -n /usr/bin/env MITMPROXY_TEST_MITMDUMP="$OUT/mitmdump" "$OUT/modeserver-integration.test" -test.run '^TestTransparentRedirectAcceptance$' -test.count=1 -test.timeout=15m -test.v
```

On macOS, build `go test -tags integration -c -o "$OUT/platform-integration.test" ./internal/platform` as the user, record the ordinary nonroot portability result, then run `sudo "$OUT/platform-integration.test" -test.run '^TestOriginalDestinationPFIntegration$' -test.count=1 -test.timeout=10m -test.v` under the 15m locked watchdog; interactive sudo is a manual runbook action, never an unattended password capture. The prepared PF test owns only its temporary anchor/state and restores it; record the filled result in the PR. No root acceptance is inferred from parser PASS.

**Existing netem experiment carrier:** existing network.yaml env and tool setup are retained; inside its netem job the exact first invocation remains `bash hack/network-gate.sh --netem-first`, then first upload, conditional validated `bash hack/network-gate.sh --netem-retry`, retry upload and final receipt check. For a local isolated Linux execution, use the same `HTTP_LOAD_TAG=h2load`, `HTTP_LOAD_PACKAGE=./internal/proxy/layers/httplayer`, `HTTP_LOAD_ENV_NAME=MITMPROXY_GO_HTTP2_LOAD`, `HTTP_LOAD_ENV_VALUE=1`, `HTTP_NETEM_TEST=TestHTTP2LoadNetem` and `NETWORK_GATE_OUTPUT` pointing at a fresh experiment root. No planner execution or credential-bearing dispatch occurs here. B's probe-local contract is `TestLoadDirectStallDiagnostics`. Add `TestLoadDirectStallMechanism` only after proving a mechanism, with an assertion-level parent failure and passing smallest repair; never create a test that passes merely because no mechanism was found. An unproved mechanism gets an evidence-only report and no B merge, never a fake product-regression PASS.

#### Final gates, security and immutable evidence

Use named native teammates with unprefixed codex definitions/no model override under the verified gateway route, model line first and wrong-model stop. Security candidates route to the fixed grok/unprefixed security-reviewer lane, never the lead. Credential operations remain with the established credential-safe executor/dispatcher and no model override; no raw credential enters reports. All commands stay at repository root, timestamps come from date in their producing invocation and no environment listing is permitted.

Per landable candidate, the unchanged CORE gates are mandatory: `bash hack/fmt.sh`, `bash hack/lint.sh` default/difftest and changed tagged variants; `go build ./... && go vet ./...`; `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` and vet; `GOOS=freebsd GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` and vet; targeted default/Windows/FreeBSD/integration/h2load gopls diagnostics covering changed Go files; whole-tree `-race -count=1` and changed-package `-race -count=5`. Full proxy `-tags integration -count=5` also runs on each candidate because its current packages/consumer already exist. Heavy gates run serially under the shared lock with complete receipts. Add that candidate's implemented focused identities and the preservation tests it touches, according to the applicability table; this replaces the blanket all-focused-rows-per-candidate rule, NOT any row's count/cpu/test timeout/post-lock watchdog. Full-tree ordinary accepted baseline skips stay governed by existing exact test-skips rules; new selected focused skips fail. Existing LDAP job is the real-service integration proof. New tests use map tables, t.Context/go-cmp, real peers, godebug/goroutine evidence as appropriate and no stubs/Only/new blanket skip. Existing pin/toolchain and load limits remain unchanged.

| FIRST unit-1 landing | An implemented, nonstub `internal/platform` address/parser/lookup/availability contract; all four portable platform identities and touched existing preservation; default/Windows/FreeBSD build+vet; scoped raw-platform security review when included | Mode handoff/lifecycle/REDIRECT and CLI rows activate on unit 2/4 consumers, not on this first prerequisite landing; root PF acceptance remains mandatory for the completed platform deliverable/final freeze |

For a grouped row whose dependent identities do not yet exist, the candidate's receipt must enumerate its exact nonempty implemented name set and the separately deferred identities with their consumer prerequisite BEFORE execution, then use that set as an exact anchored alternation in BOTH inventory and run. Keep the row's package/tags/race/count/cpu/timeouts unchanged. Already-implemented identities cannot be omitted; the full matrix's exact set is mandatory once all its consumers exist. This staging rule changes selection applicability only, not acceptance strength or native-host inventory requirements.

Every candidate touching RFC 1929/proxyauth, raw SO_ORIGINAL_DST/DIOCNATLOOK or root integration gets a scoped security review before landing; the unit-A Config.PacketDialer seam candidate ALSO requires scoped grok security review under R51-6.2. Unresolved findings block landing/closeout; green CI never substitutes for security approval.

Rebase/sign before review/push, actual bounded base, at most one pushed unlanded candidate per lane and one PR per small coherent task. Never edit a lane's tree from the lead, rewrite an approved hash or retain an approval over a changed tree. Preserve ratified narrow re-stack exceptions and fresh exact-head required checks. Keep signed intent messages <=72 columns except trailers and no plan markers; implementation attribution follows current Codex+Claude protocol. User/lead disposition for an unrelated old failure does not pre-waive a touched fixture or new mode failure.

At final source/workflow/docs SHA freeze, record immutable expected SHA and check every run headSha AND actual job checkout. Dispatch the existing native Windows producer ONCE per final freeze with its original current manifest/counts and all artifacts/digests; no second supplemental collector. New SOCKS/refusal baseline coverage comes from native CI's explicit selected rows and must not be inferred from old Windows count20 rows. One standard final Network workflow supplies seven core scenarios, complete Autobahn, HTTP3 receipts and netem first/eligible-retry evidence. If and ONLY if a unit-B PRODUCT change lands, obtain 20 distinct temporary-ref Network dispatches at the SAME frozen SHA (serial terminal-wait fallback), all terminal and separately archived. Keep first-pass/retry-pass counts separate, count no cancellation/missing upload as completion, and delete temporary refs only after independent receipt audit. No unrelated full Stress campaign or trigger restoration.

Evidence root `.omc/artifacts/p51-session/`: rulings/ownership and cause/disposition notes, focused receipts, privileged/manual/hosted manifests, security report and `reviews/verify-p51.md` plus verifier evidence/SHA256SUMS. Retain p50 reports/sidecars byte-identical. Independent verifier inputs: this approved canonical round, fixed premises only (not the lead verdict ledger), base/final source diff and exact granted-file list, upstream pin/case map/header provenance, A's initial diagnostics and queued-transport conformance, B's complete causal/no-fix report and first/retry artifacts, every candidate security report, raw focused/native/CI/manual counts and digest manifests. Verifier authors no source and independently repeats one final whole-tree race and full proxy integration count5 plus focused fixture/admission/auth/platform/diagnostic sensitivities; costly count20 campaigns may be `audit-from-receipts; not repeated by verifier`, explicitly labelled, never replaced with smoke. Verify every new test is substantive, selected, not skipped, and markers do not leak into product/history. A root skip or missing FreeBSD native test is not relabelled native success. Source/workflow/docs changes after freeze invalidate affected approvals/receipts and require a new freeze and affected dispatch/review. Finish with no pending task, clean tracked tree, immediately acknowledged worker shutdowns and state-root-safe stray .omc cleanup.

#### Deliberate RALPLAN-DR

**Mode: DELIBERATE. Principles (five):** upstream behavior is a testable pin, not inferred RFC purity; one destination capture feeds the landed layer seam; privilege/authentication fail closed and never leak credentials; boundary oracles must not depend on one unacknowledged UDP datagram; mechanism and exact-head evidence precede remediation/closure.

**Decision drivers (top three):** deliver P5 without changing the working packet-source/layer contracts; separate deterministic fixture correctness from kernel delivery/host measurements; retain numeric gate strength and independent privilege/auth assurance at bounded orchestration cost.

The following decisions compare two viable IN-SCOPE implementations each; neither option changes the mandatory deliverables, exact oracles, numeric gates or no-proof/no-merge rule. Rejection means a choice for this round based on the cited current evidence, not that the second option is impossible.

| Decision / viable in-scope option | Bounded pros | Bounded cons / evidence-based disposition |
|---|---|---|
| (a) Original destination: ioctl-first with validated pfctl fallback (CHOSEN) | Generated native tuple path after the required process-level same-peer self-check; bounded text lookup remains the fallback | Requires OS ABI/provenance and first-use synchronization, both already required by P5 deliverable 1; choose to avoid a subprocess/state scan on every validated lookup, without claiming a measured speedup |
| (a) Original destination: pfctl-first with ioctl opportunistic (REJECTED for this round) | Strict text tuple is primary as in pinned osx.py/pf.py; still generates/executes ioctl, compares it on the first usable connection and uses only validated ioctl results for optional subsequent lookup | Viable under the same mandatory ioctl/layout/self-check deliverable, but keeps the primary subprocess/state-table path even after validation; the pinned fixture parser is already required as fallback, so primary text ordering adds work rather than removing ABI obligations. Prefer ioctl-first; neither native-FreeBSD proof nor subprocess performance is inferred |
| (b) Dedicated SOCKS layer: explicit bounded state-machine parser (CHOSEN) | Named greeting/auth/CONNECT/handover states align with the pinned trickle/coalescing/premature-close expansions; stage-specific errors and credential-redacted diagnostics are directly testable | Must maintain transitions and one bounded partial frame; choose for auditable byte ownership at Recorder handover and explicit auth state, using the existing layer APIs rather than a new core parser framework |
| (b) Dedicated SOCKS layer: frame-at-a-time exact reader (REJECTED for this round) | Sequential bounded `ReadFull`-style greeting/auth/address reads can implement the same wire bytes and dedicated-layer/hooks contract with fewer explicit states | Viable with existing cancellation/watchdog and bounded fields; however the seven upstream test functions include trickle, coalesced handover and EOF at multiple stages, so a staged control-flow reader must carry implicit stage/partial-read state for diagnostics. Prefer explicit transitions to make those exact corner cases visible; no claim that exact reads cannot handle fragmentation |
| (c1) Unit A: Config-level packet-dial injection (CHOSEN) | Exactly one Config.PacketDialer field substitutes actual queued acquisition before the existing Handler registration/close path; mirrors Config.Dialer at handler.go:44–46,77–84 and keeps OpenPackets unchanged | Adds/defaults/propagates one production field in the two explicitly granted files and requires scoped grok review; choose because the hardcoded acquisition at handler_packet.go:182 is the demonstrated missing capability, while :197–229 already supplies ownership. No test-only production symbol or second registry is needed |
| (c2) Unit A: test-only packetServers registration hook via internal export_test.go seam (REJECTED for this round) | An internal-package test hook could adopt the actual queued endpoint into the SAME Handler entries/close path without adding a runtime Config field; retains real endpoints and Handler ownership, so it is a viable in-scope alternative under R51-6.3 | Requires a test-only registration/access bridge across the private proxy registry and the current external QUIC fixture package, plus explicit hook/cancel/metadata parity. Source has no such bridge: packetServers is private at handler_packet.go:126–136 and the QUIC config wrapper lives in package quic. Prefer c1's existing stream-dialer precedent and single acquisition substitution; c2 needs additional test-only production exposure and duplicated adoption coordination. No export_test.go hook is granted for the chosen implementation and no parallel registry is allowed |
| (d) Unit B: terminal-state instrumentation FIRST, then local sensitivity if needed, before the SINGLE hosted probe (CHOSEN) | Audits the actual campaign 1–3 live-state evidence and discriminates reader/control-write/native queues without first choosing a cause; positive origin credits already exclude a simple zero-credit explanation | One terminal capture may show a downstream block rather than initiation; add a deterministic local real-peer wait-cycle sensitivity when evidence warrants it, then use one unchanged hosted dispatch to validate discrimination. No extra campaign/retry or unproved merge |
| (d) Unit B: deterministic local wait-cycle reproduction FIRST, before the SINGLE hosted probe (REJECTED for this round) | Can cheaply prove a concrete control-write/reader/lock cycle with exact real-peer gates, then make the sole hosted capture narrowly test that hypothesis | Viable and retains every accepted measurement value, but the sidecars leave multiple native-queue/reader/write initiators unresolved; choosing a cycle first risks proving an artificial sensitivity unrelated to the retained stall. Prefer evidence-led instrumentation, and use local reproduction as a causal discriminator, not hosted initiation proof by resemblance |

**Scope constraints, not competing viable options:**
- pfctl-only (without generated ioctl/self-check) cannot satisfy mandatory P5 deliverable 1; moving SOCKS parsing into modeserver cannot replace the dedicated layer required by deliverable 3.
- Real-loopback scheduling alone cannot remove the two boundary fixtures' real-UDP delivery dependency; retain it as separate real-wire preservation, not the queued-transport replacement.
- Speculative window/credit/deadline/body-size/netem-queue/retry/ratio changes, ad hoc reruns and package serialization are prohibited; a proved correctness repair still preserves the values and requires the named grant/security gate.
- Generic SOCKS dependencies with unverified pinned wire behavior, metadata fallback to the listener address, duplicated lookup in layers, pfctl substring matches and classifier-side diagnostic input violate existing observable contracts. No architecture rewrite is needed.

#### Three-scenario pre-mortem and expanded test plan

1. **Wrong destination or PF ABI creates a proxy loop/wrong-origin connection.** Mitigate with generated provenance/offset checks, mapped-family decoding, same-peer ioctl/text check, strict metadata-only transparent handoff, denied lookup fail-close and Linux v4/v6 NAT with proxy-origin exemption/teardown; native versus cross-build evidence labels remain distinct.
2. **SOCKS success leaks handshake bytes or authentication is repeated/bypassed.** Mitigate with byte-trickle/coalescing/partial EOF and all upstream cases, explicit no-method/eager-error replies, accepted-client cache, correct/wrong LDAP integration, HTTP/HTTPS CLI with both curl DNS modes and scoped auth review before admission landing.
3. **Queued fixtures hide production behavior or another green retry is called a stall fix.** Mitigate with real endpoint/handler paths, queue conformance and separate real-wire coverage, initial stage/input diagnostics and full count5 gate, frozen first/retry/native state, causal gated regression before smallest fix, unproved=no behavior change and independent final evidence audit.

| Level | Required evidence |
|---|---|
| Unit | Sockaddr lengths/endian/mapped handling, exact PF vectors/nonmatches/bounds/self-check/error/cancel; all SOCKS method/auth/ATYP/reply/handover corners; proxyauth weak-client cache; queued packet FIFO/clone/empty/truncation/bounds/deadline/close; B snapshot ownership/causality/no-fix certification |
| Integration | Real Handler destination before eager dial, new modes start/stop/failure, real QUIC publication/close and DNS retained-bound unchanged oracles over queued transport, separate real UDP retransmit/forwarding; real OpenLDAP; whole-tree race and full proxy integration count5 |
| E2E / platform | Built mitmdump with curl SOCKS5-hostname/SOCKS5 HTTP+HTTPS/IP/domain; required Linux iptables+ip6tables REDIRECT original tuples; native Windows refusal; ordinary macOS parser and mandatory root ioctl/manual receipt; FreeBSD build is explicitly not execution |
| Fuzz / stress | 30-second FuzzSOCKS5Handshake, generic CI discovery, focused count20/cpu1,2,8 and exact carried-child 60/60 counts; no count or numeric acceptance relaxation |
| Observability | B actual/validated/wire counters, original typed cause, credit/write/read stage and goroutine/native queues before cleanup; A current-ID/forward-close/write/delivery evidence; root rules/teardown, per-test counts and immutable digests; no credentials/raw auth logs |

#### ADR — transparent and socks5 modes with bounded carried work

- **Decision:** implement P5 on destinationMode, capture original TCP destination in the mode server and pass immutable metadata to Handler, keep SOCKS as its own layer with existing auth hooks/validators/cache, harden only the two carried boundary fixtures with queued packets after diagnostics, and investigate the direct reference before any causal fix.
- **Drivers:** upstream/asset compatibility, narrow privilege/layer ownership, deterministic test oracles and truthful unchanged-threshold evidence.
- **Alternatives considered:** two viable in-scope choices per decision (a)–(d): ioctl-first/validated fallback versus pfctl-first/opportunistic validated ioctl; explicit state-machine versus frame-at-a-time reader within the dedicated SOCKS layer; c1 Config-level packet-dial injection versus c2 a test-only packetServers registration hook via an internal export_test.go seam, both retaining the real endpoint/Handler path; terminal-state instrumentation first versus local wait-cycle reproduction first before the sole hosted probe. The table records bounded tradeoffs and evidence-based rejection of the second option; forbidden scope changes are separate constraints, not alternate candidates.
- **Why chosen:** ioctl-first avoids retaining text lookup as the primary path after mandatory validation; explicit SOCKS states make pinned trickle/EOF/handover ownership visible; Config.PacketDialer mirrors the stream Dialer and substitutes actual queued acquisition before the existing registration/close path without a test-only production symbol; terminal evidence first avoids prematurely choosing among still-live native/reader/write causes. These choices reuse implemented mode parsing, destination selection, hookdata and pool/Recorder contracts without a public framework, and keep sidecar investigation independent of new-mode delivery.
- **Consequences:** six units with one package writer at a time (package `internal/proxy` passes serially from A to 2 by a recorded transfer); platform/manual-root and serial mode/CI queue can limit delivery. New platform ABI and test transport require substantive conformance; the single Config.PacketDialer field is the only granted production fixture change, defaults to existing behavior and adds a scoped grok gate to its candidate, not test-only follow-ups. Windows transparent refusal and SOCKS wire quirks are honest compatibility limits. B may end as a retained observation; no throughput or universal reliability improvement is inferred.
- **Follow-ups:** carry any unproved B mechanism with exact receipts; a product repair requires named grant/security and conditional 20-run evidence. A2/A3/N13 and user-directed LD-17 return remain outside; P7 entry review follows only after this round closes. No native local-mode evidence is implied.

**Confidence (planning estimates, not measurements):** performance 0.74; scalability 0.77; reliability 0.72; cost effectiveness 0.67. Existing protocol budgets are preserved; platform/manual evidence and unproved stall cause limit reliability confidence.

**Success and stop point:** all four P5 deliverables and A pass their stated actual gates; B has either a proved reviewed smallest fix with discriminating evidence or an explicit no-behavior-change observation disposition; all 18 required contexts/privileged/manual evidence complete at the frozen SHA, scoped security accepted and independent verifier PASS with no unowned blocker; docs truthful, originals retained and workers/tree/state clean. Stop before implementation until the user explicitly approves revision 15.

### P6 — netstack, WireGuard mode, TUN mode (Linux), local mode (macOS/Windows/Linux)

**Pre-tasks (3 days total)**: (a) download the `mitmproxy_macos`, `mitmproxy_windows` and `mitmproxy_linux` 0.12.11 wheels listed in §1.4, verify the macOS `.app.tar` is non-empty by extracting and listing it (unsigned CI builds ship an empty placeholder, `rs:.github/scripts/build-macos-redirector.sh:55-58`) and record versions + full SHA-256 in `go:internal/local/artifacts.go`; (b) capture IPC frames between the pinned Python mitmproxy and each redirector (control connection, `NewFlow`, `PacketWithMeta`, `UdpPacket`, Linux datagrams) into `testdata/local-ipc/` for the compatibility tests.


#### Execution: units and ownership

This section is built against main `d915d8f`. What exists: `internal/proxy/modespec` already parses `wireguard[:PATH]@port`, `tun[:NAME]`, `local[:SPEC]`, `transparent` and `socks5` (parsing accepts every mode; the server decides what it supports), so no spec grammar is written here; `internal/proxy/modeserver` admits regular, reverse and upstream and, after the P4 track's bootstrap and DNS commits land, the DNS modes and the three P4 reverse schemes; `internal/proxy/layers/modes` registers the regular, reverse and upstream top layers and has no `TransparentProxy`; `internal/proxy/layer` exposes `PacketTransport` (a virtual `net.PacketConn` per fixed tuple with context, deadlines and bounded queues) and `internal/proxy/packettransport` the UDP demultiplexer behind it, which the netstack UDP forwarder reuses as its per-tuple surface; `internal/proxy` dials origins through the pool and records byte streams; `golang.org/x/sys` is already a dependency, `gvisor.dev/gvisor`, `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard`, `github.com/Microsoft/go-winio` and `google.golang.org/protobuf` are not; the repository has no `.proto` file and no generated IPC bindings (the generated tables under `command/` and `internal/emoji/` are unrelated). Each missing integration has one owner below; a netstack unit test over raw packet builders does not close an executable mode.

| Unit | Exclusive files / integration ownership | Tests and acceptance owned | Frozen output / waits | Landing order |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| netstack core | `internal/netstack/**` (gVisor stack, forwarders, `Stream`, the semantics table, the §4 adapter file `internal/netstack/gvisor.go`), the gVisor dependency commit | Ports of `rs:src/network/tests.rs`, one contract test per semantics-table row, ICMP echo, UDP expiry with an injected clock, backpressure and close ordering | The `netstack.Stack` API (`New`, `Inject`/`Outbound` packet channel, `TCPConns()`/`UDPConns()` accept surfaces, `Stream`) frozen by its first report; no proxy import beyond `layer.PacketTransport` | First in the track; API commit, then the forwarders |
| WireGuard mode | `internal/wireguard/**` (the wireguard-go dependency commit with `internal/wireguard/device.go` as its importing adapter), `testdata/wg-test-client/**` | Config file round-trip, printed client config text, peer routing by learned source IP with first-peer fallback, the end-to-end `HELLO`/`HELLO WORLD!` row | Frozen netstack API; `TransparentProxy` from the packet-source modes unit | After netstack's API commit; end-to-end row after the modes unit's server lands |
| TUN mode (Linux) | `internal/tun/**` | Descriptor hand-over, created/fallback paths, `rp_filter`/`route_localnet`/`accept_local` end state, persistent-interface re-attach, non-Linux refusal text | Frozen netstack API; `TransparentProxy` | After netstack's API commit; the sudo rows in `packet-modes.yaml` |
| local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode | `internal/local/**` except the files the two packet-mode units name, the generated `mitmproxy_ipc.pb.go` and its checked-in `.proto` (the protobuf dependency commit: the generated file imports it; the `go:generate` line records the `protoc` invocation and the `--go_opt=M<proto>=github.com/zchee/mitmproxy-go/internal/local` package mapping, because the upstream proto has no `go_package` option), `testdata/local-ipc/**` | IPC decode over captured frames, artifact download/SHA-256/untar/mtime/`MITMPROXY_KEEP_REDIRECTOR`, intercept-spec grammar, `unavailable_reason` texts, macOS stream protocol over the Unix socket, the process-wide singleton | The IPC types and the `Redirector` interface (launch, intercept-spec push, packet in/out) frozen by its first report; nothing from netstack for the macOS stream path | Pre-tasks (a) and (b) first; IPC commit before either packet-mode unit |
| Windows packet mode | `internal/local/windows*.go` and `winio_windows.go`/`winio_other.go` (the go-winio dependency commit, build-constrained so every OS compiles) | Pipe lifecycle (message mode, first instance, reject remote, one client then listener closed), `PacketWithMeta` through netstack, checksum fill, `runas` launch kept alive across stops, the Windows manual runbook | Frozen IPC types and netstack API | After both; the manual runbook before `DONE` |
| Linux packet mode | `internal/local/linux*.go` | `sudo echo -n` probe inheriting the terminal, `sudo --non-interactive --preserve-env` launch, stderr level relay, socket path within 5 s, `UnixDatagram` packet in/out, no pid attribution, the stub-`sudo` failure text, the runner-user interception row | Frozen IPC types and netstack API | After both; the sudo rows in `packet-modes.yaml` |
| Packet-source modes and gates | `internal/proxy/layers/modes/**` (`TransparentProxy` with `remote_endpoint`, the `wireguard`/`tun`/`local` top layers), `internal/proxy/modeserver/**` after the lead's explicit `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling, `.github/workflows/packet-modes.yaml` (new), `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` rows of this track, `internal/tools/dump/**` only if a default addon is added (none is planned) | `TransparentProxy` layer tests against the P1 layer contract, mode-server admission/start/stop/`unavailable_reason` rows, the host-executable FreeBSD refusal row with an injected platform value, the privileged Linux workflow (TUN, Linux local, WireGuard end-to-end) with readiness probes, teardown traps and artifact upload | The modeserver transfer; row text from the other units | `TransparentProxy` early (P5's own transparent mode server reuses it later); the workflow after the first Linux row exists |

#### File ownership and serial transfers

- **netstack core** is the only writer of `internal/netstack/**`. Its API is frozen by its first report and approved by the lead before WireGuard, TUN and the packet-mode units build on it; a later change is announced before it is made. The stack consumes packets through a channel endpoint and hands TCP connections and UDP tuples to the proxy as `net.Conn` streams and `layer.PacketTransport` tuples, so the P1 layer stack (`TransparentProxy` → nextlayer → TLS/HTTP/TCP/UDP) runs unchanged on top of it.
- **local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode** owns the vendored `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto` copy, the generated Go (checked in, regenerated by a `go:generate` line pinned to the §4 `protoc-gen-go` version) and `testdata/local-ipc/**`; the Windows packet-mode unit owns only its `windows*.go`, `winio_windows.go` and `winio_other.go` files and the Linux packet-mode unit only its `linux*.go` files inside `internal/local`; both import the IPC types. The three units never edit each other's files; a shared helper goes into the IPC unit's files by a `BLOCKED` to the lead.
- **Packet-source modes and gates** writes `internal/proxy/layers/modes/**` except the P4 reverse-mode bootstrap files (`reverse_schemes*.go` and the P4 track's one edit of `modes.go`; its own top layers live in their own files) and, after the lead's `TRANSFER modeserver` ruling, `internal/proxy/modeserver/**`; until that ruling its mode servers are written in the unit's worktree in new files only (`modeserver/wireguard.go`, `modeserver/tun.go`, `modeserver/local.go` and their tests, an explicit new-file carve-out that touches no P4-owned `modeserver` file) against the frozen factory extension point, and are not pushed before the ruling. It is the one writer of `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` in this track (dependency rows of first commits excepted, as in P4) and of `packet-modes.yaml`; it never edits `ci.yaml` or `network.yaml`.
- No unit of this track writes `internal/h2`, `internal/h3`, `httplayer`, `dns`, `tlsparse`, `addons/**` or `cmd/**` except the mode wiring the modes unit needs in `cmd/mitmdump` for `--mode wireguard|tun|local`, which it lands in one commit after the three mode servers exist.


Deliverables
1. `go:internal/netstack/`: gVisor `stack.Stack` with IPv4/IPv6, promiscuous + spoofing NIC, default routes, `tcp.NewForwarder`/`udp.NewForwarder`, MTU 1420 (`rs:src/network/virtual_device.rs:67`), TCP 64 KiB buffers, 60 s idle timeout, 28 s keepalive (`rs:src/network/tcp.rs`), UDP 4-tuple LRU with 60 s expiry (`rs:src/network/udp.rs`), fake ICMP echo replies (`rs:src/network/icmp.rs`), a `Stream` type with the upstream `get_extra_info` keys. **Semantics table** (contract tests): close = flush then FIN, never RST; half-close requests are treated as close (as Rust ignores `half_close`); peer RST surfaces as a read error after buffered data; backpressure = `Write` never blocks, `Drain` waits for the send buffer; UDP expiry 60 s; ICMP echo answered locally. (netstack core)
2. `go:internal/wireguard/`: `device.NewDevice` over a `tun.Device` bridged to the netstack `channel` endpoint, peer config from `<confdir>/wireguard.conf` JSON `{server_key, client_key}` (base64 X25519), printed client config (`Address 10.0.0.1/32`, `DNS 10.0.0.53`, `AllowedIPs 0.0.0.0/0`, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:336-415`), `wireguard[:PATH]@port` spec (default 51820), peer routing by learned source IP with first-peer fallback (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`). (WireGuard mode)
3. `go:internal/local/`: protobuf IPC from `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto` (generated with `protoc-gen-go`, checked in) with a decode test over `testdata/local-ipc/`; artifact acquisition into `<confdir>/redirector/<version>/` on first use (or `--set local_redirector_path=`), macOS untar of `Mitmproxy Redirector.app` into `/Applications` (mtime check, `MITMPROXY_KEEP_REDIRECTOR` honoured); macOS stream mode: the proxy listens on the Unix socket `/tmp/mitmproxy-<pid>` (`rs:src/packet_sources/macos.rs:85-86`), length-delimited `InterceptConf`, per-flow `NewFlow` handshake (u32 BE prefix), raw TCP bytes, length-delimited `UdpPacket`; Windows packet mode: go-winio pipe `\\.\pipe\mitmproxy-transparent-proxy-<pid>` (message mode, first instance, reject remote, one accepted client then the listener is closed), `PacketWithMeta` in/out through netstack, IP checksum fill on reinjection, `ShellExecuteW "runas"` launch kept alive across stops; intercept spec grammar (`rs:src/intercept_conf.rs`: comma list, int = PID, substring otherwise, `!` excludes, own PID always excluded), `describe_spec` validation for `local:SPEC`; `unavailable_reason()` equivalents (on an OS without a redirector, e.g. FreeBSD, the text is upstream's `Local redirect mode is not supported on <GOOS>`, `rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:79-83`, where Rust's `std::env::consts::OS` and Go's `GOOS` both name FreeBSD `freebsd`; upstream also fails the mode start with that text, `:158-161`; on Linux it is upstream's effective-uid check, `mitmproxy is not running as root.` when the effective uid is not 0 and none otherwise, `rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:72-77`; upstream reads it only for the mitmweb `/state` field, `py:mitmproxy/tools/web/app.py:839`, and starting local mode never consults it); `local` mode is a process-wide singleton. **Linux packet mode** (added to scope by the user): launch `mitmproxy-linux-redirector` from the `mitmproxy_linux` wheel exactly the way `rs:src/packet_sources/linux.rs:29-111` does: an interactive privilege probe `sudo echo -n` (`:30-41`; the `-n` is an argument of `echo`, the probe inherits stdin, stdout and stderr so sudo can prompt for a password, and a non-zero exit fails the start with `Failed to elevate privileges`, `:39-40`), then `sudo --non-interactive --preserve-env <exe> <tmpdir>` (`:44-53`), with the redirector's stderr lines relayed to the log at the level their `[LEVEL` prefix names (`:58-83`) and its socket path read from the first stdout line within 5 s (`:103-111`); `sudo` is resolved through `PATH` (`exec.LookPath`, as `Command::new("sudo")` does); a start failure is logged as an error by `proxyserver` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/proxyserver.py:93-96`), so mitmdump exits 1 through `errorcheck` (`py:mitmproxy/addons/errorcheck.py:28-46`); the redirector chmods its sockets 0777; speak `PacketWithMeta` over a `UnixDatagram` socket pair (one message per datagram) into the netstack and reinject the same way; the intercept spec is pushed as `InterceptConf`; no `pid`/`process_name` attribution on Linux (the redirector sends `tunnel_info: None`, `rs:mitmproxy-linux/src/main2.rs`); requires root via sudo and cgroup v2 at `/sys/fs/cgroup`. (local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode for the IPC, artifacts, spec grammar, `unavailable_reason` and the macOS stream path; Windows packet mode for the pipe path; Linux packet mode for the redirector launch and datagram path)
4. **TUN mode (Linux)** (added to scope by the user): `tun[:NAME]` spec (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_specs.py:307-325`; macOS accepts only `utunN` names but the mode is Linux-only at runtime, as upstream), a `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard/tun` device bridged to the netstack `channel` endpoint, with the device setup of `rs:src/packet_sources/tun.rs:49-107,172-213` ported as follows. The proxy opens `/dev/net/tun` itself with `O_RDWR|O_CLOEXEC`, as `CreateTUN` does (`tun/tun_linux.go:552`), issues `TUNSETIFF` with `IFF_TUN | IFF_NO_PI | IFF_VNET_HDR` (the flags `CreateTUN` uses, `golang.zx2c4.com/wireguard@v0.0.0-20260522210424-ecfc5a8d5446` `tun/tun_linux.go:566`) and, once the device is configured, hands a duplicate of the descriptor to `tun.CreateUnmonitoredTUNFromFD` (`:637-660`): the proxy takes the duplicate with `unix.FcntlInt(fd, unix.F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC, 0)` (a plain `unix.Dup` has no close-on-exec flag, and the same process spawns children: the Linux redirector through `sudo`, the `browser` addon, the console's external editor), sets it non-blocking itself with `unix.SetNonblock` (closing the duplicate if that fails, because the function returns before wrapping it at `:638-641`), passes the duplicate, and always closes its original descriptor afterwards; from the call on, the duplicate belongs to the `os.File` the function creates (`:642`), which closes it on `Close` (`:482-499`) or, on the error returns at `:651-658`, through its finalizer, so the proxy never closes a descriptor number twice; the device stays attached because the attachment belongs to the open file description, which the duplicate shares. The unmonitored device starts no netlink or hack listener, so it never emits `EventUp` (only those listeners send it, `:88,181`), and the netstack bridge must not wait on `Events()`. `tun.CreateTUN` and `CreateTUNFromFile` are not used, because `CreateTUN` leaks the descriptor when `TUNSETIFF` fails (`:567-569`) and `CreateTUNFromFile` always calls `setMTU`, which needs `CAP_NET_ADMIN`, and on that error closes only the netlink socket (`:626-629`). *Created path*: MTU 65535 (`SIOCSIFMTU`), address `169.254.0.1` (`SIOCSIFADDR` with `unix.Ifreq.SetInet4Addr`) and link up (`SIOCSIFFLAGS` with `IFF_UP`) through `golang.org/x/sys/unix` ioctls (`tun.rs:50-56`) issued on an `AF_INET` datagram socket opened for the purpose, not on the TUN descriptor (the `SIOCSIF*` ioctls name the interface in the `Ifreq`; wireguard-go's own `setMTU` opens such a socket, `tun/tun_linux.go:231-245`), then `configure_device` (`:89-107`): `disable_rp_filter` ported in full (`:174-213`: write 0 to the interface's `rp_filter`; if `conf/all/rp_filter` is not 0, raise every other interface's `rp_filter` to max(all, interface) and then write 0 to `conf/all/rp_filter`; values are compared after trimming the trailing newline, whereas upstream compares the raw file contents with `"0"` at `:185`, so its early return never fires and only the end state, which is the same, is reproduced), `route_localnet=1` (`:93-98`) and `accept_local=1` (`:99-106`, which upstream sets so that injected packets with a local source address such as 127.0.0.1 are accepted), each failure logged as upstream does. *Fallback path*: when `TUNSETIFF` or a configuration ioctl fails with `EPERM` and `NAME` is set, the proxy closes its descriptor and attaches to the existing persistent interface with `TUNSETIFF` alone, leaving MTU, address and `/proc` values untouched, as upstream retries with a default configuration (`:67-83`); without `NAME` the `EPERM` is returned. The fallback happens before the hand-over, so the descriptor the proxy closes there has never been duplicated, and no descriptor leaks on the fallback; on other OSes the mode start fails with upstream's text `TUN proxy mode is only supported on Linux` (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/tun.rs:46-47,80-83`). (TUN mode)
5. `TransparentProxy` in `go:internal/proxy/layers/modes` (moved here from P5 deliverable 2, because this round runs before P5): the top layer that takes the original destination from `remote_endpoint` and hands the connection to nextlayer exactly as the reverse layer does with its configured target; mode servers for `wireguard`, `tun` and `local` reusing it; `cmd/mitmdump` wiring for the three modes. (Packet-source modes and gates)

Time-boxes: netstack 5 days, WireGuard 4 days, TUN 2 days, Linux packet mode 3 days, macOS stream mode 4 days, Windows packet mode 5 days; WireGuard and TUN may land alone if any local-mode sub-task overruns by more than 3 days (S3), but the round remains incomplete until every required P6 criterion is met or the user explicitly changes the scope.

Acceptance criteria
- netstack: ports of `rs:src/network/tests.rs` cases (`ipv4_udp`, `ipv6_udp`, `tcp_ipv4_connection`, `tcp_ipv6_connection`, `receive_icmp4_echo`, `receive_icmp6_echo`) pass against the Go stack with raw packet builders; the semantics table has one contract test per row; the UDP 60 s expiry and the 28 s keepalive are proved with the injected clock, never by sleeping; a TCP stream whose peer stops reading stalls only itself (`Write` returns at once, `Drain` waits) while another stream on the same stack completes. (netstack core; all three OSes)
- WireGuard e2e: `testdata/wg-test-client/<platform>` run with `test.conf` against `mitmdump --mode wireguard` plus an upper-casing TCP/UDP addon receives `HELLO` and `HELLO WORLD!` (mirrors `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_servers.py`); run on linux-x86_64 (`packet-modes.yaml`) and macos-aarch64 (`ci.yaml`'s existing macOS test job can run it without privileges, because the WireGuard listener is a plain UDP socket) in CI, windows-x86_64 manually. (WireGuard mode)
- local (macOS): manual runbook on macOS 12+ — artifact download verified by SHA-256, extension approval, `local:curl` intercepts `curl https://example.test` from a terminal, `pid`/`process_name` present in `client_conn` extra info; result recorded as a dated note in the PR (no CI). (local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode)
- local (Windows): manual runbook on windows-2025 runner or a local VM — UAC prompt once, intercept of a `curl.exe` process, packets round-trip through netstack; checksum correctness checked with a packet capture; IPC decode test over the captured frames passes in CI on all OSes. (Windows packet mode; the IPC decode row belongs to the IPC unit)
- local (Linux): on the ubuntu-26.04 runner (passwordless sudo, cgroup v2), the test setup writes an `/etc/hosts` entry mapping `example.test` to the runner's primary non-loopback IPv4 address, where the test origin listens (the redirector's handling of loopback destinations is not verified, so the test does not depend on it); `mitmdump --mode local:curl` run as the runner user plus `curl http://example.test:<port>/` from a shell produces an HTTP flow whose `server_conn.address` is that address and port and whose client extra info has no `pid`; a `wget` in the same session is not intercepted; with a stub `sudo` that exits 1 placed first on `PATH`, `mitmdump --mode local` exits 1 and the log contains `Failed to elevate privileges`; the password-prompt path (a sudoer with a password typing it at the probe) is manual-only, run once and recorded in the PR. (Linux packet mode; the rows run in `packet-modes.yaml`)
- TUN (Linux): on the runner, the test runs under sudo: `mitmdump --mode tun` creates an interface; with an `httptest` origin on `127.0.0.1:<port>` and **no route added**, `curl --interface <tun-name> http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` (curl binds to the device, as upstream's test binds its socket with `SO_BINDTODEVICE`, `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/test_mode_servers.py:445-447`, and upstream's docs use `curl --interface tun0`, `py:docs/src/content/concepts/modes.md:392`) yields a flow with `server_conn.address` `127.0.0.1:<port>` and curl receives the origin's response; no route is added because a route for the destination in the main table would also carry mitmdump's own outbound dial (eager by default) into the TUN, so each connection would spawn another; the interface's `rp_filter` and `conf/all/rp_filter` read back as `0`, `route_localnet` and `accept_local` as `1`; with a persistent `tun0` pre-created by `ip tuntap add dev tun0 mode tun user <runner user>` and configured by the test, `mitmdump --mode tun:tun0` run without sudo attaches to it, writes no `/proc` value, and a second start after the first one stopped attaches again; on macOS `mitmdump --mode tun` exits 1 and the log contains `TUN proxy mode is only supported on Linux`. (TUN mode; the sudo rows run in `packet-modes.yaml`)
- A host-executable unit test of the mode server with an injected platform value `freebsd` asserts the start refusal, the exact log text `Local redirect mode is not supported on freebsd` (`rs:mitmproxy-rs/src/server/local_redirector.rs:79-83,158-161`) and the exit classification (exit 1 through `errorcheck`); a `GOOS=freebsd go build ./...` cross-compile is separate evidence that the tree builds there, not execution of that binary; the IPC unit owns only the `unavailable_reason` function's own test; the mitmweb `/state` check lives in P8. (Packet-source modes and gates)

#### Executable gate recipe and testability

Deterministic netstack, IPC and mode-server tests run under `-race` on ubuntu-26.04, xcode-27 and windows-2025 through `ci.yaml` unchanged. The privileged rows (TUN under sudo, Linux local mode as the runner user with passwordless sudo, the WireGuard end-to-end row on Linux) run in the new required job of `packet-modes.yaml` on ubuntu-26.04: it records kernel, `ip`, `sudo` and cgroup versions, pre-creates the persistent `tun0` for the re-attach row, writes the `/etc/hosts` entry for `example.test`, installs EXIT/INT/TERM traps that delete the interfaces, hosts entry and `/proc` values it changed, and uploads logs and the redirector's stderr on success and failure; a missing privilege, kernel feature or artifact is a failed required job, not a skip. The macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks are manual, dated and recorded in the pull request before the unit's `DONE`; their IPC frames feed the decode test that does run in CI. The per-commit gate is the P3 gate; before a unit's final push, `internal/netstack` at `-race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, the IPC decode and mode-server rows at `-count=200`, and the netstack packet-builder fuzz target (the IPv4/IPv6/ICMP parsers of the injected path) for 30 s. Every new regression row is red on its parent commit in a detached scratch overlay and green on the unit's tip.

| IPC decode, artifact acquisition, spec grammar, unavailable_reason, singleton | Absent | Captured frames from pre-task (b); SHA-256 and untar rows with fixtures; the `unavailable_reason` text rows | local IPC, artifacts and macOS stream mode; all three OSes |
