### The follow-up round after P4+P6 (revision 11)

**What the round is.** Nine units, each funded by one of the user's closeout decisions of 2026-10-09 (ledger `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/rulings-p46.md`, "USER CLOSEOUT DECISIONS batch 1" 22:02 JST, "batch 2" 22:27 JST, "batch 3" 22:29 JST, re-appended 2026-10-10 00:17 JST after the `.omc` recovery): fixture hardening (batch 2, item 1); HTTP/3 abort-table and QUIC close parity as one unit (batch 2, items 2 and 3); deterministic real-HTTP/3 acceptance (batch 2, item 4); WireGuard multi-peer routing (batch 2, item 5); the HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path (batch 3, item 9); DNS transaction-ID eviction (batch 3, item 11); CI skip visibility (batch 3, item 14); the macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks (batch 1, item 6); the upstream apply-ACK proposal (batch 1, item 13). It is built against main `6161318`. P5 follows this round. The round's mechanics are those of the P4+P6 round (one lead, one `flock` gate lock, one unlanded pushed commit per lane, rebase before review, exact-hash evidence, draft pull requests re-opened after every landing, dependency commits first and alone, the lead never rewriting a pushed candidate), with the additions under "Round rules" below.

**Truth paragraph against `6161318`.** What exists and what is open, per unit:

- *Carried flaky rows (LI-2 family, CI-31, CI-38).* The one-rerun policy is in force: a carried row that fails in a full-tree gate is rerun once in isolation, the original log retained. The rows: `go:internal/proxy/packettransport/transport_test.go` `TestTuplePackets/preserves_boundaries` and `/empty_is_not_EOF` (`:135`, `:140`, "first datagram socket delivery did not complete", 30 s); `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/dns_authoritative_test.go:67` `TestReverseDNSAuthoritativeTransports/UDP` ("DNS exchange: read udp ... i/o timeout; request hooks=1 response hooks=1 error hooks=0 origin replies=1"); `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/local_flow_test.go:111` `TestLocalIPCOrigins/UDP_real_origin` ("read udp4 i/o timeout"); `go:internal/proxy/proxytest/streaming_test.go:228` `TestLargeBodyStreaming/success:_upload` ("waiting for test event hung", 30 s, CI-31, the proxytest event-collector family); `go:internal/netutil/freeport/freeport_test.go:27` `TestGetFreePort/success:_TCP_and_UDP` on `windows-2025` (CI-38: "bind: An attempt was made to access a socket in a way forbidden by its access permissions", a Windows excluded port range; the helper tries `attempts = 64` candidates, `go:internal/netutil/freeport/freeport.go:17`). These are six subtest rows in five families (two `TestTuplePackets` subtests, the DNS, local, streaming and freeport rows). The verifier saw two of them fail under full-tree load and pass alone (NB-3); `integ.sh` needed its single rerun on four consecutive landings. Every one of them is a test-fixture or helper row; no production defect has been attributed to them.
- *HTTP/3 abort table and QUIC close parity.* `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/quicgo_abort_test.go:33-44` holds the twelve-row consumer abort table (`request`/`response` × `buffered`/`streamed` × `reset`/`close`/`reset then close`). LG-2 (`request streamed close`, a Linux CI ordering failure) was fixed test-only in `28c6d0d`, which the verifier could not discriminate on its parent (NB-9). LG-3 (`response streamed close`: a missing error hook observed once in an expanded race run, `.omc/handoffs/lane-p46-h3-integration.md:272`) is open with no reproduction and an unanswered cause-preservation question (which close cause reaches the consumer when the remote application close and the local cleanup race). In `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic`, `TestQUICConsumerBorrowing/protocol abort` (`consumer_test.go:71`) failed under cold full-tree load as an idle timeout instead of application code `0x102`; the keepalive fixture of `57e23bd` (hypothesis B, idle expiry under load) landed test-only and the original finding stays open. Transport-level `CONNECTION_CLOSE` parity (`rs:` has none; upstream `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/quic/_raw_layers.py:290-302` forwards code, frame type and reason) is unrepaired: quic-go v0.63.0 exposes only application `CloseWithError` for caller-originated closes, and the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/proxy/layers/quic` says so; `close_forward_test.go:95` ("remote transport close") tests only the tracer's diagnostic formatter with a synthetic `qlog.ConnectionClosed` event, so no real transport-close forwarding row exists; normal cleanup sends application code 0 with an empty reason (`go:internal/proxy/layers/quic/quicgo.go:54-56`) and forwarding maps only a remote `ApplicationError` (`:523-528`), never a transport code. LG-1 (`TestQUICWireCloseBeforeContextPublication` readiness timeouts under load) is closed as "cause unproven, fixture hardened" (`0d82e26`) and is not reopened by this round.
- *Real-HTTP/3 acceptance.* `py:test/mitmproxy/proxy/layers/http/test_http3.py` (at `3368a0a`) defines eighteen test functions: `test_ignore_push`, `test_fail_without_header`, `test_invalid_header`, `test_simple`, `test_response_trailers`, `test_request_trailers`, `test_upstream_error`, `test_http3_client_aborts` (parametrised), `test_rst_then_close`, `test_cancel_then_server_disconnect`, `test_cancel_during_response_hook`, `test_stream_concurrency`, `test_stream_concurrent_get_connection`, `test_kill_stream`, `test_receive_stop_sending` (parametrised), `TestClient.test_no_data_on_closed_stream`, `TestClient.test_ignore_wrong_order`, `test_early_server_data`. The engine-level map `.omc/handoffs/p46-h3-upstream-map.md` (eighteen Python rows, `:56-73`) marks the hook-dispatch and flow-level sequences as integration-owned and untested (`:65-66`, `:72`). The closeout counted six full-case sequences covered, one covered with a documented divergence and eleven uncovered (closeout item 4); the case-reconciliation archive that listed them was lost in the `.omc` deletion of 2026-10-09 22:31 JST, so the unit re-derives the list before it writes a test.
- *WireGuard.* `go:internal/wireguard/server.go:33`: `New` configures exactly one client peer (R46-16); the mode server in `go:internal/proxy/modeserver` (`wireguardSource`) prints that peer's client configuration. Upstream's Rust engine routes by learned source IP with a first-peer fallback (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`), and upstream's Python mode server writes one `{server_key, client_key}` pair (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/mode_servers.py:336-415`), so the routing algorithm follows the Rust engine, and supplying more than one peer additionally requires the Go-only `client_keys` configuration extension that unit 4 defines; the legacy two-key format and the default generated file stay unchanged.
- *HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path.* `go:internal/h2/owner.go:219-224`: a framer `http2.StreamError` for a stream the owner knows is handled by `o.cancel(...)` with the framer's code, which takes the stream-cancel path; a present identity whose frame the framer rejects therefore follows the same path as a peer reset. The P3.5 review named this a retained, unaddressed boundary (round preamble above); no regression row exists.
- *DNS transaction-ID retention.* `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:49` keeps `flows map[int]*flow.DNSFlow`; `:123-128` inserts a flow per message id and nothing deletes an entry until the connection ends, exactly like upstream's `self.flows` (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:61,67,164-169,180`). Up to 65,536 entries per connection, each message bounded at 1 MiB, no aggregate budget (security review, inherited debt, closeout item 11). The user kept parity for the P4+P6 round and funded eviction now.
- *CI skip visibility.* `go:.github/workflows/ci.yaml:64` runs `go test -race -count=1 ./...` without `-v`, so a `t.Skip` prints nothing and the job stays green whether the row ran or skipped. Four capability skips fire when IPv6 loopback is absent: `TestBothListenerDualStack` (`go:internal/proxy/modeserver/both_failure_test.go:56,61`), `TestStandaloneDNSWireTransports/IPv6_*` (`dns_standalone_test.go:39`), `TestBoundPacketFactoryRollback` (`factory_binding_test.go:21`); they PASS with 0 SKIP on macOS and on the remote Debian host with `-v`, and the GitHub runner behaviour is unobserved (NB-4, R46-147). The other eleven added skips are `GOOS` guards, one env opt-in (`MITMPROXY_TEST_REDIRECTOR_DOWNLOAD=1`) and one Windows helper guard (NB-5). `hack/` holds `network-gate.sh`/`network-gate_test.sh` and `packet-modes-gate.sh`/`packet-modes-gate_test.sh`, the pattern a new gate script follows.
- *macOS and Windows local mode.* `docs/compat.md` under `internal/local` says that installed-app and system-extension capture on macOS and UAC/WinDivert process attribution on Windows "remain manual acceptance boundaries"; no runbook document exists under `docs/`, and the P6 criteria that asked for dated manual runs recorded in a pull request were not executed (closeout item 6). The user chose a manual runbook over dedicated runners.
- *Readiness contract.* §3.4 item 8: the barrier works without an ABI change; the user chose to keep it and to propose an explicit apply-ACK upstream (closeout item 13).

**Lane composition and ownership.** The nine units run on five lanes so that the two `httplayer` units and the two small protocol units do not contend; the lead may split a lane when a unit's time-box is at risk, never merge two units' files into one commit.

**File ownership and serial transfers**

- Frozen for the round: `go:internal/proxy/packettransport/*.go` production files, `go:internal/proxy/layer/**`, `go:addon/hookdata/**`, `go:internal/h3/**` (the engine is complete; a needed change is a `BLOCKED` to the lead), `go:internal/netstack/**`, `go:internal/local/**`, `go:internal/tun/**`, `go:dns/**`, `go:tlsparse/**`. Lane A's grant on `transport_test.go` is the one test-file exception inside a frozen package, and it is written here so that NB-10 does not recur.
- `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer` is written by lane B (production: `quicgo.go`; tests: the abort-table files) and lane C (new test files only, plus the `h3test` helper package). Lane B lands first; lane C rebases onto lane B's landed commits and never the reverse. Neither touches `http1.go`, `driver.go`, `stream.go` or the HTTP/2 adapter.
- `go:internal/proxy/modeserver` is written by lane A (two test files and their helpers) and lane D (`wireguard*` files, by grant); the two sets do not overlap, and each lane rebases onto the other's landed commit when its own rebase conflicts in a shared helper.
- `go:internal/proxy/proxytest` is written by lane A (`streaming_test.go`, the event collector helpers) and lane C (new `http3_*_test.go` files only); lane A lands first.
- `docs/compat.md` and `docs/dependencies.md` have one writer, lane E; lanes B and D supply their row text in their `DONE` reports, and the lead relays it. `docs/runbooks/**` and `docs/proposals/**` are new directories owned by lane E.
- No unit edits `cmd/**`, `addons/**`, `internal/proxy/handler*.go`, `pool.go`, `poolconn.go` or `runner.go`; the P4+P6 hardening unit's files are closed.

**Deliverables and acceptance criteria**

1. *Fixture hardening (lane A).* Deliverables: (a) a recorder watchdog for the packet-transport and UDP fixtures: the fixture peer reports positive admission and delivery signals (first-tuple admitted, first datagram delivered) on channels the test selects on, and a hang detector with a goroutine dump replaces the silent 30 s timeouts at `transport_test.go:135,140`, `dns_authoritative_test.go:67` and `local_flow_test.go:111`; (b) a request-hook barrier in proxytest's event collector so that `TestLargeBodyStreaming` waits for the request hook to be observed before it asserts streaming, and the collector's "waiting for test event hung" reports which event it waited for and which events arrived; (c) `freeport` skips Windows excluded port ranges: on Windows the helper reads the excluded ranges once (`netsh int ipv4 show excludedportrange protocol=udp` and `protocol=tcp`, parsed; a parse failure falls back to the current behaviour with a logged note) and never proposes a candidate inside them, keeping `attempts = 64` and the retained-candidate rule of R46-57. Acceptance: each of the six subtest rows (both `TestTuplePackets` subtests named individually in the stress manifest) runs `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` clean in isolation and two fresh full-tree `go test -race -count=1 ./...` runs under the lock pass without a rerun, on macOS (lead gate) and on the remote Debian host (`-count=20` on the named rows), with the exact commands and logs in the lane note; the freeport row passes on the exact-head `windows-2025` job twice (two pushes or one rerun requested explicitly as evidence, labelled as such); a parent-red row exists for the request-hook barrier (the collector's old timeout path, reproduced with an injected delay) and for the excluded-range skip (a fixture excluded range that the old helper proposes); the recorder-watchdog rows are preservation rows and are labelled so. When all six subtest rows have landed, the lead retires the one-rerun policy for them by name in the ledger; a later failure of one of them is a blocking finding, not a carried row.
2. *HTTP/3 abort cause and QUIC close parity (lane B).* Deliverables: (a) LG-3: a deterministic reproduction of the missing error hook on `response streamed close` using the tracer-gated close-cause publication window already used by `close_forward_test.go` (quic-go `Config.Tracer`, no library patch), then a repair that preserves the first observed close cause (the remote application error observed by the datagram reader wins over the local cleanup close, as `go:internal/proxy/layers/quic/quicgo.go:523-528` already does for raw QUIC; `0d82e26` hardened only a readiness fixture) so the consumer's error hook fires exactly once with that cause; (b) the `protocol abort` row: a cold-load reproduction attempt with the fixture-hardening load profile (two fresh full-tree `-race` runs after lane A's first landing) and the native-event capture of the P4+P6 investigation; if the row still fails only as an idle timeout under load, the unit may convert the assertion to "application code `0x102`, or the idle timeout when the abort packet is provably undelivered" only under an evidence contract stated in the test: the proxy-side qlog shows the `CONNECTION_CLOSE` frame sent, the client-side packet capture (the native-preserving `UDPConn` wrapper of the P4+P6 investigation, `ReadBatch` path) shows the datagram not received, and the client's reader goroutine is proved runnable (a goroutine dump at the timeout shows it blocked in the socket read, not stalled behind the race scheduler); endpoint qlog non-receipt alone does not distinguish packet loss from a stalled reader and is not sufficient; the lane note labels the outcome as a fixture decision with that evidence attached; (c) transport-close parity: a decision, not necessarily a repair. The unit checks the quic-go v0.63.0 public API for a way to emit a transport-level `CONNECTION_CLOSE` with a caller-chosen code and frame type (`quic.Conn.CloseWithError` is application-level; `closeWithTransportError` is unexported, `connection.go:2189`); if none exists, the compat row stays and the unit adds a real peer-induced transport-error certification row (a frame-level peer that provokes a transport error, not a synthetic tracer event) asserting the current behaviour: the opposite peer receives the cleanup application close with code 0 and an empty reason (`quicgo.go:54-56`) and the original typed transport diagnostic is preserved in the returned error; any mapping of a transport code onto an application code is a separate behavioural change and is not what this row pins. Acceptance: the LG-3 row is red on `6161318` (or on the unit's parent) through the tracer gate and green on the unit's tip, at `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`; the abort table (`quicgo_abort_test.go`) passes `-count=200` and the two full-tree runs without a rerun; the `protocol abort` row passes the same profile or carries the labelled fixture decision with the qlog evidence; the compat row under `internal/proxy/layers/quic` is either rewritten (repair) or confirmed by the new pinning row (decision), and the lane note says which. The time-box is strict: a fourth day without a deterministic LG-3 reproduction ends the unit with "unproven, pinning rows landed" as the disposition, reported to the user.
3. *Real-HTTP/3 acceptance (lane C).* Deliverables: (a) `docs/compat.md` gains no row; the unit first re-derives the case map: for each of the eighteen upstream functions, one row stating covered (which Go test), covered with divergence (which `docs/compat.md` row explains it) or uncovered, recorded in the lane note and in a comment block at the head of the new test file; (b) one deterministic Go test per uncovered sequence in `http3_upstream_cases_test.go`, driven by frame-level peers in `h3test` (a client that sends HTTP/3 frames over a real quic-go connection to the proxy's HTTP/3 listener, and an origin that answers at the frame level; modelled on the existing in-package helpers `newHTTP3RoutingListener` and `startHTTP3RoutingPeer` in `go:internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/quicgo_routing_test.go:45,70` and the `internal/h3` plus `qpack` peers of `quicgo_abort_test.go`, which lane B owns and lane C does not edit; `proxytest` has no HTTP/3 row today), with the addon hook sequence asserted through `addon/addontest` and the flow state through `flow`; each test names the upstream function and line range it maps. Acceptance: eleven new rows (or the re-derived count, with the difference explained in the lane note), each green on all three OSes at `-race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`; for a behavioural regression the lane overlays only the new test and helper files onto the parent's unmodified production code and records the actual failure, while a sequence that is already correct is labelled a certification row (allowed: these rows exist to pin behaviour, not to find defects); a deliberate production mutation is separate mutation-sensitivity evidence and is never reported as red on parent; the two timed HTTP/3 rows of `network.yaml` stay green on the landed head; the Windows `-count=50 -cpu=1,2,8` evidence comes from lane E's stress matrix job (the baseline `count=1` jobs are not stress evidence); `test_http3_client_aborts` maps onto the existing abort table and is not duplicated.
4. *WireGuard multi-peer routing (lane D).* Deliverables: (a) a contract commit that extends `go:internal/wireguard` `Config` with an ordered peer list (the existing `{server_key, client_key}` file stays valid and yields one peer; a second form with `client_keys: [...]` is a Go-only extension recorded in `docs/compat.md`, never written by default; precedence is defined: a file carrying both `client_key` and `client_keys` is rejected with a named error, `client_keys` must be non-empty and free of duplicates, and the first entry is the fallback peer), and `New` with the engine's routing: outbound packets are routed to the peer whose learned source address last sent from that address, with the first configured peer as the fallback for addresses no peer has used yet (`rs:src/packet_sources/wireguard.rs`); (b) the mode server prints one client configuration per peer, each with a distinct `Address` (the first peer keeps `10.0.0.1/32`, the n-th gets `10.0.0.n/32`; the DNS and `AllowedIPs` lines are unchanged); (c) `testdata/wg-test-client` runs two clients with distinct keys and addresses, driven by the extended `TestWireGuardExecutable`. Acceptance: the routing table is a unit row with an injected packet sequence (learn, route, fallback, re-learn after a client changes address); the end-to-end row runs two clients concurrently against `mitmdump --mode wireguard` through the DumpMaster harness and each receives its own upper-cased reply (`HELLO` and `HELLO WORLD!` per client, no cross-delivery), on the Linux privileged job and the macOS job; the single-peer configuration file produces byte-identical behaviour to `6161318` (preservation row); the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/wireguard` is rewritten by lane E from lane D's `DONE` text: the single-peer deviation paragraph is replaced by the routing description and the Go-only multi-key configuration form.
5. *HTTP/2 malformed-present framer path (lane D).* Deliverable: in `go:internal/h2/owner.go:219-224`, a framer `StreamError` for a present stream is classified: when the framer rejected a frame of a stream the owner has an identity for, the stream fails with a `ProtocolError`-derived stream error that names the framer's reason (not the peer-reset path), the owner queues a `RST_STREAM` with the framer's code, and the flow's error records "malformed frame" rather than "reset by peer"; the idle-stream and unknown-stream branches are unchanged. Acceptance: a frame-level peer sends `WINDOW_UPDATE` with increment zero on an already-open non-zero stream, the one stream-scoped framer rejection `x/net/http2` v0.60.0 produces (`frame.go:1015-1020`; a HEADERS pad length larger than its payload is the other, `:1106`); the flow's error text names the malformed frame, the peer receives `RST_STREAM` with `PROTOCOL_ERROR`, a sibling exchange completes, and the new test overlaid onto the unmodified parent fails on `6161318` (the parent records the reset-by-peer text) and passes on the tip; a DATA frame with a bad padding length is a connection error in the framer (`frame.go:669-675`) and a CONTINUATION-order violation is disabled by `AllowIllegalReads` (`go:internal/h2/endpoint.go:293`), so those two stay connection-error preservation rows and are not this stream-error row; the P3 budget, intercept and stream-stall rows pass as preservation rows; the reset-source labels of the P3.5 round stay distinct (a peer reset still says so).
6. *DNS transaction-ID eviction (lane E).* Deliverable: `dnslayer` evicts a transaction-ID entry when its exchange completes (the response has been forwarded and the `dns_response` hook has run, or `dns_error` has fired) and keeps it while the request is outstanding, so a retransmitted request with the same id before the answer reuses the flow (as upstream), and a response for an evicted id follows the path upstream and main already take for a never-seen id: a new flow is created, `dns_response` fires with no request on the flow, and the response is forwarded to the client (`py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:96-101,164-169`; `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:123-128,271-283`), so eviction changes nothing a hook can observe for a well-behaved exchange; because the map holds the owner's only reference to a flow, eviction first finalises the flow the way shutdown does today for the remaining entries (`Resume`, `Live = false`, `dns.go:88-96`) and only then deletes the entry, so an intercepted or paused flow is never dropped while live; an aggregate bound of 1,024 outstanding (unanswered) entries per connection closes the connection with the invalid-message diagnostic when exceeded (UDP: the tuple is evicted). Acceptance: rows for complete-then-evict (the entry is gone after the response hook; asserted through the owner's map size in a package test), retransmit-before-answer (one flow; `dns_request` fires once per received request, so twice, and the retransmission is forwarded upstream again, exactly as upstream's `handle_request` and main's `owner.request` do, `py:mitmproxy/proxy/layers/dns.py:71-93`, `go:internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go:204-205`; one `dns_response`), late response after eviction (a second flow with no request, one `dns_response`, the response forwarded, identical to the never-seen-id row on main), the 1,024 bound on TCP and on a UDP tuple; the existing `test_dns.py`-derived rows pass unchanged; `-count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` and the fuzz target 30 s clean; the `docs/compat.md` row under `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` records the deviation (upstream retains every id until shutdown; the Go layer evicts completed exchanges and bounds outstanding ones, retransmissions preserved).
7. *CI skip visibility (lane E).* Deliverable: the `ci.yaml` test step runs `go test -race -count=1 -json ./... | hack/test-skips.sh`, where the script reads the `go test -json` event stream in shell without `jq` (no new dependency), correlating each `skip` action that carries a non-empty `Test` field with the `output` events of the same package and test (the skip message arrives as escaped `Output` lines interleaved with other tests' output, so the reader keys on `Package` and `Test`, not on an `Action` regex alone), prints every per-test skip with its package, test name and message, classifies it (`GOOS` guard, env opt-in, privilege guard, capability, unknown) from the message text, writes the counts to the job summary, reports package-level `no test files` skips separately, preserves the `go test` pipeline exit status (`set -o pipefail`), and exits non-zero for every non-GOOS-guarded per-test skip, unknown messages included, as the user's decision words it (ledger `:1202`: "failing on non-GOOS-guarded skips"); the env opt-in row (`TestAcquirePinnedArtifacts`, `MITMPROXY_TEST_REDIRECTOR_DOWNLOAD=1`) and the privilege-guard rows (`internal/privfile` foreign-owner rows, `privfile_unix_test.go:30-35`, which skip when the test cannot chown) fire on every runner today, so the unit lands only after the user rules on an explicit allowlist for them (§10 item 18); until that ruling the script is landed in report-only mode behind a `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` workflow variable defaulting to off, and the enforcing switch is the unit's last commit. `hack/test-skips_test.sh` feeds recorded `go test -json` samples for the unknown, privilege, capability, environment and `GOOS` cases plus a `no test files` package and asserts the counts and the exit status. Acceptance: an exact-head CI run on the unit's commit prints the counter for all three OSes with zero capability skips and zero unknown skips; `shellcheck` and `actionlint` clean; the shell test runs in the lint job; the four NB-4 rows are listed by name in the script's capability class; the enforcing run fails a synthetic unknown skip (a temporary test in a scratch branch, evidence only).
8. *macOS and Windows local-mode runbooks (lane E).* Deliverable: two runbooks that a person follows on a macOS 12+ machine and on a Windows 11 or `windows-2025` VM: prerequisites (the pinned 0.12.11 artifacts and their SHA-256, extension approval on macOS, UAC on Windows), the exact `mitmdump --mode local:curl` invocation, the `curl` command, what to observe (a captured flow whose `server_conn.address` is the origin; `pid` and `process_name` are native transport `GetExtraInfo` values on the local stream, `go:internal/proxy/modeserver/local.go:292,305`, and not fields of the stock mitmdump client flow, because `connection.Client` carries no extra-info field and the handler builds the client from addresses only, reading `remote_endpoint` alone, `go:internal/proxy/handler_source.go:27-41`; the runbook therefore supplies an exact approved diagnostic procedure for reading them, such as a debug log line the proxy emits at the local stream's admission, and if no such procedure exists on main the runbook marks process-attribution verification BLOCKED, keeps that native evidence absent and never claims the CLI exposes those fields), how to capture packets for the Windows checksum check, teardown, and a results block (date from `date`, OS build, artifact digest, pass/fail per step) that the person fills and commits. Acceptance: the two files exist, pass the docs gate, and name every step the P6 criteria asked for (macOS: artifact SHA-256, extension approval, `local:curl` intercept, pid/process_name; Windows: one UAC prompt, `curl.exe` interception, netstack round-trip, checksum check by capture); the manual execution is a user action outside the round's completion criterion and is recorded, when it happens, by committing the filled results block.
9. *Upstream apply-ACK proposal (lane E).* Deliverable: a proposal document that states the problem (the redirector publishes its endpoint before rules are applied; `rs:src/packet_sources/linux.rs` and `rs:mitmproxy-linux/src/main2.rs` at `51fe2b7`, with line references verified against the clone), the Go-side barrier that works around it (§3.4 item 8), the proposed IPC change (an `InterceptConfApplied` message, or a sequence number echoed back, in `rs:src/ipc/mitmproxy_ipc.proto`), its backward compatibility (a Go frontend that does not receive the ACK within the barrier window keeps the barrier), and the Go adoption plan (one `internal/local` unit after the upstream release that carries it). Acceptance: the document exists under `docs/proposals/`, passes the docs gate, and ends with the exact text of an upstream issue the user may file; nothing is sent to upstream by this round.

**Executable gate recipe and testability**

**Historical revision 11 gate, amended by ratified closeout decisions.** The round used the P3 gate under the lock, labelled parent-red/certification/preservation evidence, exact-head required jobs and the dedicated stress rows. The stress workflow was subsequently extracted to `.github/workflows/stress.yaml`: Linux and Windows run one job per manifest row; macOS runs every row serially and records all failures before failing at the end. By LD-17 it is now **manual-only (`workflow_dispatch`)**, not a pull-request or main-push gate. The ratified historical return condition is “until more of the port is implemented / when the implementation settles.” The user kept that condition at the approval of revision 12 (2026-10-11): the triggers return when the user directs it; the planner's next-round/no-new-product-finding criterion was not adopted, and revision 12 does not restore the triggers. Baseline test jobs still run `-race -count=1`, not acceptance stress counts. Revision 12 requires the table's local count-based evidence and one lead-dispatched hosted stress run on its final head; historical Windows stress evidence is not silently asserted for a new candidate. Dependency changes retain the OpenLDAP integration and `govulncheck` additions, and messages retain the signed/72-column/no-plan-marker gate. Remote Debian measurements set `umask 022` before invoking tests, use an isolated temporary toolchain/cache tree, and record `date`, kernel, toolchain, head and exact counts.

| Criterion | Status against `6161318` | Required change / evidence | Owner / execution |
|---|---|---|---|
| Six carried subtest rows (five families) deterministic; one-rerun policy retired for them | Carried, one-rerun policy | Watchdog, barrier, excluded ranges; `-count=200` + two full-tree runs + remote `-count=20`; Windows job twice | Unit 1; macOS lead gate, remote Debian, Windows job |
| LG-3 cause preserved; `protocol abort` disposed; transport-close decision | Open, open, unrepaired | Tracer-gated red/green; cold-load profile; API check and pinning row | Unit 2; all three OSes |
| Eighteen upstream HTTP/3 sequences mapped, uncovered ones tested | 6 + 1 + 11 (to be re-derived) | Case map in the lane note and test header; one row per uncovered sequence | Unit 3; all three OSes |
| Learned-source routing, first-peer fallback, two-client e2e | Single peer | Contract commit, routing rows, two-client e2e on Linux privileged and macOS jobs | Unit 4; Linux privileged + macOS CI |
| Present-identity framer rejection classified | Reset path | Red-on-parent row, `RST_STREAM PROTOCOL_ERROR`, sibling isolation | Unit 5; all three OSes |
| Completed-exchange eviction, retransmission kept, 1,024 bound | Parity (never evicts) | Five rows, fuzz, compat row | Unit 6; all three OSes |

**Round rules** (continuations of the P4+P6 round, restated so the charters can quote them)

- Workers are spawned as unprefixed agent types (`executor`, `test-engineer`, `writer`, `critic`, `verifier`, `grok-security-reviewer`) with no model override; the security review runs on `grok-security-reviewer` with the tool allowlist (Bash, Read, Glob, Grep, SendMessage); the verifier pass is the one lane the user approved on sonnet. Instructions to workers are English; reports to the user are Japanese.
- Every time a worker or the lead writes comes from `date` in the same command; a worker never lists its environment (`printenv`, `env`, `set`) in any transcript.
- A stray `.omc` directory is deleted only after its resolved absolute path is compared with the resolved state root and found different; the P4+P6 closeout deleted the state root through a trailing-slash mismatch, and the recovery is `.omc/artifacts/p46-session/closeout/RECOVERY-README.md`.
- One commit per landing, fast-forward only, the gate under the lock, exact-head CI on the hash that lands, draft pull requests re-opened after every landing, rulings and landing cues sent over both the inbox and the lane's socket. A lead push or re-stack on a lane's behalf follows §3.4 item 11.
- The one-rerun policy continues for the six carried subtest rows (both `TestTuplePackets` subtests, the DNS, local, streaming and freeport rows) until unit 1 retires each of them by name in the ledger; no other row may be rerun.

**Round pre-mortem (five scenarios)**: (1) the fixture rows pass in isolation and keep failing under load because the cause is a lost datagram, not admission timing: unit 1's positive delivery signals distinguish the two, and a proven loss is reported as a product finding to the lead, not fixed in the fixture; (2) LG-3 never reproduces: the strict four-day box ends the unit with pinning rows and the honest label; (3) the uncovered-sequence count changes on re-derivation: the lane note explains the difference and the user decides whether the count matters; (4) multi-peer routing changes single-peer behaviour: the preservation row on the existing configuration file catches it; (5) the skip counter fails a job for a skip nobody expected: that is the intended behaviour (unknown per-test skips fail closed and need classification before landing), and the report-only mode behind `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` lets the lead see every class on all three runners before the enforcing switch lands.

**Verification at the end of the round**: one scoped security re-confirmation on `grok-security-reviewer` over `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` (eviction changes retention semantics under attacker-chosen ids), `internal/wireguard` (routing keyed by untrusted source addresses), `internal/h2/owner.go` (the new error path) and the `httplayer` abort changes; then one verifier pass on sonnet with the same criteria as the P4+P6 round (every regression row red on its parent and green on main, certification and preservation rows labelled, the stress rows at the counts above, main CI and both evidence workflows green on the landed head). The round is complete only on the final immutable head.

### The closeout follow-up round (revision 12)

**Outcome (revision 13).** COMPLETE. All units 1–8 landed, followed by action pins `ff7e501` and netem classifier hardening `2de7987`; main at closeout is `2de7987` (11 landings). Verifier Addendum 1's final confirmation is PASS with findings, 0 blocking; the corrected independent security re-review is ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES and explicitly supersedes the earlier REJECT. Stress run `38110004980` is green: 16 jobs, 15 artifact archives and 21 OS-row executions on actual checkout `ff7e501`. It is not a dispatch on `2de7987`: the verifier explicitly proved unchanged workflow/manifest/default-race inputs, while separately verifying the changed h2load/netem paths and fresh final-head required workflows. Final `2de7987` has 38/38 successful check runs and all 18 required identities green after the recorded Windows rerun; the original DNS failure remains retained. Source evidence: `closeout-decisions.md:3,34-38,50-54`, `rulings-p48.md:168-186`, `reviews/verify-p48.md:188-232` under `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/`. The section below is execution history, not unfinished work.

**Context.** p48: closeout follow-ups is a small hardening and evidence round against main `8e6319f`; it does not start P5 or redesign the port. Revision 11 is complete: nine units DONE-accepted, 17 landings, verifier PASS with findings (0 blocking, 14 non-blocking open), security delta ACCEPT-WITH-NOTES. Its final product fix is `77cde39`, preserving completed HTTP/3 responses during cleanup; `8e6319f` is the manual-only stress-workflow descendant. The source inputs are the final p47 closeout, rulings, verifier Addendum 3 and the delta-security report. The following descriptions carry their evidence without requiring the reader to open the ledger.

**Objectives and guardrails.** Must have: bounded learned routing; no log callback while the demux lock is held; secret-free peer-count refusal; correct DNS bound wording; test-only memory-measurement stabilisation with the streaming correctness assertions intact; symmetric client/server FIN coverage; exact allowlisted skips visible and enforcing on all three test jobs; unchanged netem comparison preserved in a separate automatic required Network gates job; one final hosted stress receipt. Must not have: dependency/toolchain bumps, routing-policy changes beyond the approved finite LRU retention and the existing miss fallback, source spoofing/authentication changes, QUIC cleanup redesign, lowered throughput/heap limits, hidden retries, new blanket skip classes, restored automatic stress triggers, production edits for N15/N17 or NB-24 without a new lead grant, credential handling N13, native runbook execution or upstream filing by workers.

**Truth against `8e6319f`.** `demux.go:143-170` logs some drops under `socketDemux.mu`; pre-validation drops occur before that lock. `router.go:23-35,53-55` and `stack_device.go:98-105` retain every successfully admitted source for the listener lifetime. `Config.PeerKeys` validates forms, keys and duplicates but not count (`config.go:163-186`); rendering increments whole IPv4 addresses (`:229-232`). DNS checks map size before allocating (`dns.go:125-136`) and removes a successfully forwarded response after hooks (`:278-305`), retaining hook-suppressed responses. `TestLargeBodyStreaming` is `integration && !race`, forces GC only at its baseline and samples heap-object bytes during/after transfer without GC (`streaming_test.go:189-210`), so garbage can inflate the peak; the recorded failure was 65,521,144 bytes against 50 MiB under parallel package load, and both subtests passed 10/10 alone. `TestTrailersFollowActualFIN` matches quic-go's entire cancelled-Close text with its stream id (`conformance_test.go:145-147`). The FIN-preserving branch in `requestState.fail` applies to both roles (`request.go:82-91`), but the direct regression row covers server responses only (`lifecycle_test.go:131-206`). `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` defaults to off via a repository variable (`ci.yaml:51`) and every non-GOOS skip is rejected when switched on (`test-skips.sh:177,195`). `network.yaml` still invokes `TestHTTP2LoadNetem`, whose 256 MiB transfer has matched 16 MiB windows and an 80% floor; two failures occurred in the direct reference's await before the ratio was computed. `stress.yaml` is workflow_dispatch-only, splits Linux/Windows by row, and runs macOS rows serially with failure accumulation.

#### Task flow and exclusive ownership

Four implementation lanes may work in parallel; the gate lock serialises resource-heavy tests and landings. The bottleneck is the lead's CI/gate queue, not code authoring. Each lane keeps at most one pushed, unlanded candidate. The preferred order below is deterministic; later independent work can be prepared locally while waiting. The CI lane lands the netem relocation before skip enforcement so the performance row runs independently with its bounded visible retry while the existing core Network gates remain unchanged.

| Order | Unit / owning lane | Exclusive files and scope | Dependency / candidate shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Demux logging M1 / WireGuard | `internal/wireguard/demux.go`, `demux_test.go`, optional new `demux_logging_test.go` | No dependency; first small production candidate, alone |
| 2 | Netem placement NB-16 / CI-docs | `.github/workflows/network.yaml`, `.github/workflows/stress.yaml` (return-condition comment only), `hack/network-gate.sh`, `hack/network-gate_test.sh`; `internal/proxy/layers/httplayer/http2_load_netem_test.go` (phase/outcome diagnostics and netem-only waits, unchanged measurement contract), optional new sibling netem diagnostic/classifier tests; optional named `hack/netem-gate.sh` and its shell contract only if reuse needs it | No product dependency; isolated harness/workflow candidate; no production or threshold/window/size/deadline modification |
| 3 | Bounded WireGuard configuration/routing L1 + L4 / WireGuard | `router.go`, `stack_device.go`, `config.go`, `router_test.go`, `config_test.go`, existing multi-peer tests or new sibling contract tests | Rebase after order 1; one cohesive resource-bound candidate |
| 4 | HTTP/3 fixture and client-role coverage N15 + N17 / HTTP3-tests | `internal/h3/conformance_test.go`, `lifecycle_test.go`, optional new sibling helper test file | Production `request.go`/`endpoint.go`/`quic.go` read-only; one test-only candidate |
| 5 | Streaming heap measurement NB-24 / DNS-streaming | `internal/proxy/proxytest/streaming_test.go` only | Independent; one test-only candidate |
| 6 | DNS retained-entry contract L3 / DNS-streaming | `internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer/dns.go` (constant/comment wording only; no admission algorithm change), `retention_owner_test.go`, `retention_test.go` or one new sibling certification file | Serial after order 5 on this lane; supplies compat text to CI-docs |
| 7 | Skip allowlist and enforcement A1 / CI-docs | `hack/test-skips.sh`, `hack/test-skips_test.sh`, optional checked-in exact-row allowlist under `hack/`, `.github/workflows/ci.yaml` | After order 2; parser+allowlists first, switch `TEST_SKIPS_ENFORCE` on in the same candidate once contract checks pass; fresh exact-head CI mandatory |
| 8 | Closeout wording / CI-docs | `docs/compat.md`, `docs/dependencies.md` only for current setup-go wording if present; `stress.yaml` return-condition comment belongs in order 2 | After source units' DONE text; docs-only candidate, single compat writer |
| Final | Hosted evidence N14 / lead, then security and verifier lanes | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/` ledger and receipts; no source ownership | After all candidates land; one workflow_dispatch Stress run on final head, then independent review/verify |

Other source files remain frozen. WireGuard's `server.go` or a mode-server file is not a routine grant: report BLOCKED if validation bypass is discovered rather than widening the edit set. All three `PeerKeys` consumers must be inspected to establish that count validation occurs before engine allocation. `docs/plans/mitmproxy-go-port.md` is applied once by the lead after consensus approval; implementation workers do not amend this contract. The docs lane receives plain-language compat text from the owning lane; it does not independently change product behavior.

#### Detailed deliverables, acceptance and evidence

**1. Demux logging (M1, WireGuard lane).** Count drops while the routing operation owns its lock, then invoke slog after release; pre-lock malformed-packet paths remain valid. Preserve one count per rejected recipient/packet as on the parent, and power-of-two logarithmic reporting. Keep the counter atomic for both locked and pre-lock drop paths; capture each returned count that crosses a power of two, including multiple crossings during one fanout operation. Preserve debug level, the exact existing drop message and `dropped` field; tests compare fields as well as count/timing. Snapshot the count used by the log so a later drop cannot change the reported value. No asynchronous logger queue or new goroutine is required. This fixes lock coupling; synchronous logging can still block the `dispatch` caller and the socket-reader loop after the lock is released, which is not claimed fixed here.

Acceptance: a debug-enabled, deliberately gated slog handler signals entry then blocks; concurrently registering a legitimate local handshake index completes while that handler is blocked. A bounded deadline detects deadlock and the fixture releases/joins all goroutines on either result. Cover unknown receiver index, initiation fanout with a full recipient queue, and known-index full queue (the three locked drop sites); malformed pre-lock input is preservation. Assert exact drop totals and log counts at powers of two, collision fanout/index expiry and normal authentication behavior unchanged. Evidence: parent-red on unmodified candidate parent for the gated-lock row if mechanically observable (expected; report an honest non-reproduction if not), green tip; new focused rows `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` (600 executions per top-level row) and entire `internal/wireguard -race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`; exact-head required CI. No source packet becomes a peer registration.

**2. Netem placement (NB-16, CI-docs lane).** Chosen option: isolate the netem throughput scenario as a separate **automatic** Linux job in `.github/workflows/network.yaml`, named **`netem throughput`**, running on the workflow's existing pull-request/main-push triggers (and manual dispatch). It is **required**: the lead adds this one context to the main ruleset at landing, retaining the existing 17 (18 thereafter). It is not a `stress.yaml` job and not a `hack/stress-rows.txt` row; that manual race-only cross-platform workflow is unchanged apart from its explicit LD-17 return-condition comment. Keep `TestHTTP2LoadNetem` and its 80%/256 MiB/16 MiB-window contract unchanged. The Network gates default path still runs transfer, unrelated-response, HTTP/3 transfer/unrelated-response, both heap rows, SSE latency and the complete Autobahn manifest. Split or mode-select the existing gate harness so the dedicated netem entrypoint requires only its actual inputs, compiles the same h2load binary, establishes a fresh network namespace, configures lo with 50 ms netem and TCP maxima 64 MiB, and always removes/restores its resources. No Docker/Autobahn run is required for the netem-only mode. Keep `isolated network gates` and every existing ruleset identity unchanged. Use a 30-minute job budget above two 600-second measurement attempts, setup and artifact upload; each invocation retains its test/process deadlines. No `continue-on-error` is added.

Acceptance: default Network gates no longer require/execute the netem test, but still fail if any remaining required scenario is missing/skipped. The separate automatic Linux job runs the netem-only mode non-race, in isolation, and archives exact SHA, tool versions, namespace/qdisc/sysctl state, bytes, direct/proxy durations and ratio. At most two measurement attempts, classified mechanically by phase/outcome markers; an unclassified nonzero exit or generic `operation did not complete` is never retry permission. The narrow test-only grant adds stable diagnostic markers for `setup`, `direct-handshake`, `direct-warmup`, `direct-transfer`, `proxy-setup-warmup`, `proxy-transfer`, `measurement-complete`, and harness `restoration`. Each transfer-start marker follows successful request submission and completed readiness/warmup, identifies its leg and stream, and carries expected bytes=268435456/windows=16777216. Netem-specific wait diagnostics retain existing deadlines and report `measured-transfer-timeout` only from the measured completion wait while the peer/connection remains live; a peer/connection error is a protocol failure, not a timeout eligible for retry. Shared load-peer/session helpers and non-netem tests stay unchanged; if they cannot provide the distinction within this grant, report BLOCKED for a named diagnostic-only grant instead of guessing from stack text.

The harness first checks transcript validity/completeness for the reported outcome, then successful namespace restoration, then decides retry. Eligibility is exactly either (a) an explicitly attributed `measured-transfer-timeout` after transfer-start, with no setup/handshake/warmup/protocol/cleanup error, or (b) `measurement-complete` with both legs' exact byte counts, finite positive durations, matching windows, computed ratio<0.8, and all other assertions passing. A timeout receipt lists the timed-out leg, last validated phase, expected bytes/window, available progress/duration fields and explicit `unavailable` for unmeasured actual bytes, direct/proxy completion durations or ratio; it never fabricates 0 or a ratio. Completed-below-floor receipts cannot omit any completed measurement field. Missing, duplicate, out-of-order or contradictory markers, a whole-test/containing-command timeout without a valid measured-timeout outcome, setup, handshake, warmup, protocol, transcript, upload/tee or restoration failure fail immediately with no retry. A restoration failure invalidates eligibility even when the ratio was below floor.

Only an eligible first attempt receives one fresh-namespace retry; retain separately named first/retry logs and validated receipts. A valid retry pass is labelled **`accepted flaky measurement under the approved two-attempt policy`**, not 2/2 success or identical single-attempt evidence. This is an explicit changed evidence-acceptance policy, despite the unchanged numeric floor. Two eligible failures fail the required job; any invalid attempt fails it immediately, even if a later attempt happens to pass. The required status must never go green on an invalid first attempt. Shell contracts inject phase/outcome transcripts and command outcomes to prove first-pass success, both eligible failure kinds/retry-pass, measurement-fail/retry-fail, setup/handshake/warmup/protocol errors, absent fields, malformed/out-of-order markers, restoration failure and producer/tee failure. No real network service is mocked. `bash -n`, shellcheck, actionlint and shell contracts pass. Evidence: fresh exact-head CI/Network/Packet required jobs on the candidate, including the new automatic netem job, and the corresponding final-main job; no parent-red product claim (placement policy change). Landing step owned by the lead: save the current ruleset context list, add only `netem throughput` with its actual check application/context identity after the candidate's fresh check passes, read back all 18 contexts with no deletions/renames, then fast-forward through the gate; stop if a required context is absent or still pending. The macOS manual-stress serial race loop must still run all rows after an early failure, with its accumulated status. Change its comment to retain the **ratified historical** general return condition. The next-round/no-new-product-finding condition was offered by the planner and declined by the user at the approval of revision 12 (2026-10-11); the triggers return when the user directs it. Do not restore schedule/PR triggers in this round. Do not reinterpret a 30-second direct-reference timeout as proof of low proxy throughput.

**3. Bounded WireGuard routing and peer count (L1/L4, WireGuard lane).** Add a **4096-entry LRU per packetRouter/listener**, shared by all configured peers, with O(1) lookup, admission refresh, destination-hit refresh and eviction. A source entry is learned only after successful authenticated plaintext admission through `stack.Inject`; retain the existing lock ordering that prevents an immediately generated reply racing source publication (`stack_device.go:98-105`). Last admitted sender wins for a duplicate source. A hit or successful relearn becomes most recent; a miss does not create an entry; the oldest is evicted on the 4097th distinct source. One peer can displace another peer's resident route: there is no per-peer quota or reservation. An evicted/missing address uses the existing first-configured-peer fallback until that source next sends a successfully admitted authenticated packet, at which point it is relearned for its sender. 4096 is a per-listener aggregate, not 4096 per peer. 4096 is deliberately above 255 printed peers (more than 16 learned addresses per peer on average) while bounding map/list state independently of attacker-chosen inner address diversity. It is not a bandwidth, connection or global-process limit. Use standard map/list primitives or existing compatible infrastructure; do not add the MPL LRU dependency or a runtime option in this round.

Reject `client_keys` longer than **255** in the shared `PeerKeys` validation before per-key expensive work, cloning, client rendering or peer-engine creation. Error wording is fixed and secret-free (`client_keys must contain at most 255 peers`); use a named validation error with period-ended godoc, without echoing key material. Preserve conflicting-form precedence, empty-list and duplicate/invalid-key errors for existing valid-sized input. Keep the generated legacy file, 4096-byte file read bound, public API and first client text unchanged. This is a configured-peer boundary restoring the approved single-/24 `10.0.0.n/32` address plan: the 256th peer silently crosses to the next /24 because `Addr.Next` increments the entire address. Do not claim the parent rendered invalid IP at peer 256.

Acceptance: table rows insert 4096 distinct valid IPv4/IPv6 sources, then one more; assert map/list size exactly <=4096 after each operation, the correct victim, refresh-on-hit/relearn, last sender wins, missing/evicted destination peer 0 **and restoration of sender ownership only after a new admitted authenticated packet from that evicted source**, no allocation on misses, no learning on rejected/invalid input. Existing encrypted two-peer acceptance passes without cross-delivery. Programmatic Config rows for 1, 255 and 256 distinct valid keys: 255 accepted with 255 distinct valid `/32` addresses, last `10.0.0.255/32`; 256 refused by `PeerKeys`, `ClientConfig` and `ClientConfigs`, returning no partial configs; error has no key substring. Caller inspection/contract proves engine creation validates first. File limit unchanged; large file still fails as too large, not a fabricated 255-key JSON row. Evidence: new bounded-retention regression row red on parent (unbounded map) and 256 rejection contract row red on parent (missing newly adopted limit), both green tip; the latter labelled policy-regression, not invalid-IP reproduction. Entire wireguard `-race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, deterministic admission/lookup interleavings `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`, existing encrypted e2e on exact-head macOS WireGuard and privileged Linux jobs. Docs text states bounded retention/LRU fallback and peer cap.

**4. HTTP/3 test portability and symmetric cleanup (N15/N17, HTTP3-tests lane).** Confine quic-go cancellation-Close text tolerance to **one test helper** using `strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "close called for canceled stream")`; tolerate it only for the late-DATA-rejection branch. Nil is accepted normally; an arbitrary close error or any error in delayed-FIN still fails. Preserve the following `Reset`/`ErrCodeFrameUnexpected` assertion; never treat text matching as the product assertion. Helper sensitivity rows cover current text, another stream number, trailing detail, unrelated close error and strict-branch rejection.

Add a client-role sibling of `TestEndpointCancellationPreservesResponseFIN`: the upstream client endpoint sends completed request HEADERS/DATA/FIN, retains a response/read receipt so cleanup cannot rely on StreamDone, then `client.CancelStream` is called. Assert its FIN-closed write half is not reset (inspect QUIC write-half error as the server-role row does), pending receipt is invalidated, and the origin gets full request bytes plus FIN. A paired incomplete-request row keeps reset code `ErrCodeRequestCancelled`; a sibling exchange still completes. Join real peers, no sleeps or dependency patch. Production request cleanup stays unchanged unless a new concrete failure is reported and separately granted.

Evidence: prefix-helper rows are fixture-sensitivity/certification, not a production defect claim; completed-client-request row is green on current parent as new coverage, and red on **pre-FIN-fix `5d9a4a4`** with only the new test overlaid (label this historical parent-red explicitly). Incomplete-client-request and existing server-role rows are preservation. Entire `internal/h3 -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8` (600 runs per row), late-DATA test `-race -count=600 -cpu=1,2,8` (1800 per subtest), symmetric cleanup rows `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`, httplayer `^TestHTTP3Upstream -race -count=50 -cpu=1,2,8`, and exact-head baseline/integration jobs. This coverage does not close NB-15 or NB-23 without reproduction/attribution.

**5. Streaming heap sampling (NB-24, DNS-streaming lane).** Choose GC-stabilised live-retention sampling **without widening the 50 MiB bound**. Keep `integration && !race`, the 100 MiB streamed body, 32 KiB fixture chunks, all early/final-byte/hook barriers, byte checks, RawContent=nil checks and cleanup unchanged. After setup, force GC and capture the baseline. In the sampler, force GC before every read (periodic, explicit barrier-triggered and terminal), preferably using `/gc/heap/live:bytes` consistently; retain the maximum post-GC live value, not the pre-GC heap-object peak. **Before `readBody` calls `releaseTail`, while the last byte is still held at the sender and the completion hook has not fired, request an explicit sampler reading and wait for its acknowledgment.** The acknowledgment identifies the completed post-GC reading and its value; merely observing a ticker tick is insufficient. Both upload and response rows must record at least one acknowledged in-transfer sample at this barrier, selected with context/watchdog failure diagnostics, then release the tail. Keep the existing 30-second stage watchdog and all streaming/timing assertions unchanged. Serialize periodic/explicit/terminal reads in the same sampler goroutine; do not call concurrent GC/read over the same sample storage. Saturating/non-negative subtraction prevents unsigned underflow if the final live heap shrinks below baseline. Explain that the criterion bounds live retained streaming state, not cumulative allocations or throughput; forced GC affects timing, so do not use its durations as performance evidence.

Acceptance: both upload/response rows still prove prefix delivery before final byte, exactly one completion hook and no retained body; measurement uses the same post-GC metric baseline/peak and fails at >=50 MiB. A **passing sensitivity row** uses the same sampler and barrier request/acknowledgment, allocates a body-sized object only after the post-GC baseline, and keeps that allocation reachable for the sampler's monitored lifetime through its acknowledged in-transfer and terminal readings (use an explicit ownership reference and `runtime.KeepAlive` after the final acknowledgment). Assert measured growth **>=50 MiB**, proving the unchanged normal-row rejection boundary catches retained body state; the sensitivity row succeeds on that assertion rather than leaving a deliberately failing normal test. Release the allocation only after sampling is stopped/joined. Both normal subtests log the acknowledged held-tail sample and still require <50 MiB. Keep all sensitivity code test-only in the granted file, with no silent skip. Reproduce the recorded old measurement under the same non-race package-load profile if possible; if no parent failure reproduces, label it fixture/measurement hardening, report failures/N and the original 65,521,144-byte receipt, and never fabricate parent-red. Evidence: focused non-race `go test -tags integration -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8 -run '^TestLargeBodyStreaming$' ./internal/proxy/proxytest` (600 runs x two subtests), two fresh whole `./internal/proxy/...` integration runs exercising normal parallel package load under the gate lock, remote Debian named row `-count=20` with `umask 022`, exact-head Linux/macOS proxy integration. No simultaneous benchmark campaigns, no ratio/bound adjustment to get green.

**6. DNS retained-entry semantics (L3, DNS-streaming lane).** Choose documentation/certification rather than counting unanswered requests only: the existing resource bound should include every retained flow, including a response a hook suppresses. Rename the private constant and amend its diagnostic/comment to describe **1024 retained transaction-ID entries per client connection/UDP tuple**, keeping size, admission location and fail-closed invalid-message behavior unchanged. Use `dnslayer: retained DNS transaction limit exceeded (1024)` as the accurate diagnostic. No unbounded requestless-flow bucket and no new counter with a separate definition of “outstanding.”

Acceptance: package-owner rows verify (a) successfully forwarded requestless response is finalised/deleted; (b) a response hook clearing Response retains the entry; (c) a new id at 1024 retained entries refuses before new flow creation or hooks; (d) same-id retransmission still reuses the existing flow even at capacity; (e) normal completion/error finalises before deletion; (f) hook-mutated ids never change the immutable map key. Bound/late-response/retransmit rows cover TCP and UDP. Compat table wording says retained entries, explicitly describes hook-suppressed response retention, and does not claim ordinary unsolicited responses accumulate. Evidence: certification/preservation (the chosen admission behavior already exists), entire dnslayer `-race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8`, bounded real-socket fuzz invocations `-fuzztime=200x -parallel=1` per target after recorded TIME_WAIT drain (<1000 or 90 seconds measured with date), plus exact-head 30-second fuzz-smoke CI and required jobs. The diagnostic wording is a documented correction, not a new connection-closing behavior.

#### Long-count command matrix and watchdog budgets

Every row below runs **after gate-lock acquisition**, with an explicit Go test timeout and a larger admitted-shell watchdog. Start the outer watchdog inside the lock so lock queueing does not consume the measurement budget. The containing budget includes compilation, process cleanup and log/receipt finalisation; Bash's foreground-call limit is not a test timeout. Record the exact expanded command, toolchain, host, immutable SHA, date-derived start/end, exit status, top-level/subtest totals and failures/N. No simultaneous resource-heavy campaigns.

| `go test -tags integration -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8 -timeout=90m -run '^TestLargeBodyStreaming$' ./internal/proxy/proxytest` | 90m | 105m | Non-race, 600 top-level runs x two subtests; generous GC-assisted margin, not a claimed measured duration |
| `go test -tags integration -count=1 -cpu=1 -timeout=10m -run '^TestStreamingHeapSamplerSensitivity$' ./internal/proxy/proxytest` | 10m | 15m | Passing retained-allocation sensitivity using the same acknowledged sampler |
| `go test -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8 -timeout=60m ./internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` | 60m | 75m | 600 executions/test; explicit margin above the recorded 577s package run |
| `go test -race -count=1 -timeout=60m ./...` | 60m | 75m | Fresh full-tree gate; verifier repeats once at final SHA |
| `go test -count=5 -timeout=30m <changed-package-list>` | 30m | 35m | Each candidate expands its exact scoped package list in the receipt; no broad rewrite of unit counts |
| `go test -tags integration -count=1 -timeout=60m ./internal/proxy/...` | 60m | 75m | Non-race normal parallel-package profile; streaming unit runs two fresh invocations, verifier one fresh final-head invocation |
| Remote Debian: `umask 022; go test -tags integration -count=20 -timeout=30m -run '^TestLargeBodyStreaming$' ./internal/proxy/proxytest` | 30m | 35m | Run from the isolated remote repository root; same named row, non-race, no added cpu multiplier |
| `go test -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8 -timeout=30m -run '^TestEndpointCancellationPreservesRequestFIN$' ./internal/h3` on each declared parent overlay and tip | 30m | 35m | Historical `5d9a4a4` red, current-parent certification and tip; overlays add only needed tests/helpers, production unmodified |
| `go test -run '^$' -timeout=10m -fuzz '^FuzzTCPMessages$' -fuzztime=200x -parallel=1 ./internal/proxy/layers/dnslayer` and separate identical `FuzzUDPMessages` invocation | 10m each | 15m each | Existing bounded fuzz mode after measured TIME_WAIT drain; not a replacement for count200 |

For the alternation row, the executable command is `go test -race -count=200 -cpu=1,2,8 -timeout=30m -run '^TestEndpointCancellationPreserves(Response|Request)FIN$' ./internal/h3`; the escaped pipe in the Markdown cell only preserves the table structure. All other commands execute as printed after expanding the one changed-package list.

GC-assisted streaming calibration: the first focused count=5 run measures wall time with `date` and validates the acknowledged held-tail sample/sensitivity. It is calibration, not acceptance. Keep the count200/cpu1,2,8 acceptance invocation unchanged; 90m is a conservative initial test budget and 105m is its containing budget. If calibration projects more than 60m for acceptance (less than 30m margin), report to the lead and review a budget increase **before** the count200 campaign; preserve the existing per-transfer watchdogs, tags, counts, cpu set and assertions. This does not authorize raising limits or accepting a timed-out run.

Any Go timeout, outer-watchdog kill, truncated/partial log, incomplete test tally or unjoined fixture yields FAILED/INCOMPLETE evidence, never a count receipt. Retain its log and record an owner. Budget increases are reviewed explicitly without decreasing counts or silently relabelling an earlier timed-out attempt; the new invocation gets a distinct receipt.

#### Gate recipe, landing mechanics and worker models

All implementation/author/planning/ordinary review/verifier workers use the **codex-* agent definitions on `claude-gpt-6.1-sol-fast`** through gateway-enabled native tmux teammates; the scoped security reviewer uses **`claude-grok-4.7[1m]`** and declares its actual model. Every first report states the actual model; a misrouted lane stops before writes. No `model: opus` substitution for implementation or verification is silently made. Credential-bearing operations receive no model override and are routed through the lead's established credential-safe worker mechanism; no raw secrets enter plan/evidence. All worker commands run from the repository root with absolute paths, timestamps from `date`, no environment listing or other process command lines.

Per candidate: format (`gofmt`, gofumpt, modernize, goimports-rereviser via `hack/fmt.sh`), lint default and difftest tag, build, target vets, full-tree `-race -count=1`, changed packages `-count=5`, proxy integration, plus the unit counts above, under the single gate lock. Keep Go 1.27.2/module-selected toolchain and pinned dependencies. New Go rows use `tests := map[string]struct{...}` and `t.Context`, go-cmp, real peers, period-ended godoc. Local windows cross-compile/vet is not native execution. Exact-head CI supplies Windows baseline coverage; Windows count evidence, where required, comes from the final hosted manual stress receipt. Do not claim baseline count=1 as stress.

Each candidate has one PR, a signed commit (or the ratified docs/workflow/test-only-lower stack form), and fresh exact-head required-check runs before fast-forward landing through the lead's gate. The main ruleset initially requires **17 status checks**: snapshot their actual names/contexts at round start, not an inferred job count. Unit 2 adds **`netem throughput`** as a new required check, making **18** from that landing onward; the lead performs the context addition/readback before fast-forwarding only after its fresh exact-head job is green. No existing context is dropped/renamed, and manual Stress jobs are not made PR-required. LD-8's reduced re-gate may retain initial local stress receipts for a patch-identical disjoint re-stack, but changed-package or go.mod overlap repeats that package's counts. Because stress is off PRs, such receipts must already exist locally; the final hosted run is additional evidence, not a prerequisite substitute retroactively claimed on every earlier PR. LD-12 run reuse is recorded as history, not permission to omit fresh candidate required checks in this round. A red landed-main confirmation becomes an owned finding; it is never erased by later green runs.

Rebase with `git r` before review/scan/push, bounding it by the candidate's actual base. Lead never git-writes in a lane worktree or rewrites a pushed candidate. Signed messages are <=72 columns (trailers excepted), state the reason in ordinary words and contain **no unit/NB/LD/N identifiers, lane names or plan markers**; the same exclusion applies to code/comments/workflows/harness scripts. Code authored by execution lanes carries `Co-Authored-By: Codex <noreply@openai.com>` beside `Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; review-only lanes add no authorship trailer. A changed head invalidates that head's review. Original failed logs and all labels are retained. The former six-row one-rerun policy was retired at p47 closeout; the only planned retry is the explicitly visible netem measurement retry, not general CI reruns.

After each DONE acceptance shut down that worker immediately; recover a missing report with one liveness ping then its transcript, not indefinite sleep-polling. After every worker has acknowledged shutdown/timed out, inspect nested `.omc` directories, resolve absolute paths against the legitimate state root, move any substantive plan/research/handoff to the root, then remove only stray hook state. Never delete the state root. Finish with a clean tracked tree and no pending lane work.

#### End-of-round verification artifacts, producers and duties

The lead freezes the final source/workflow/docs SHA before hosted dispatch or approval passes; no landing or amend proceeds while that evidence is being gathered. Record expected SHA in the ledger and dispatch the ref resolved to that exact head, then independently compare both the workflow run `headSha` **and each job's checkout SHA** to it. A mutable ref name is not proof: a mismatch invalidates that receipt and requires a correctly pinned dispatch. Parent overlays may add the declared new test/helper files but keep parent production unmodified. Review-only lanes do not produce commits or authorship trailers.

| Artifact | Exact path | Producer | Required content / consumption |
|---|---|---|---|
| Round rulings / failures / waivers | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/rulings-p48.md` | Lead | Append-only decisions, immutable source SHA, actual model attestations, owned failures/observations, explicit accepted waivers, landing/context changes, dates from the command writing each record |
| Required contexts before/after unit 2 and final readback | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/evidence/required-contexts.json` | Lead | Main ruleset id and actual application/context identities; original17, addition of only `netem throughput`, readback18; check run ids/head SHAs/conclusions for final main; no missing/renamed original contexts |
| Hosted stress and automatic netem receipt manifest | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/evidence/hosted-receipts.json` with `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/evidence/SHA256SUMS` | Lead | Expected immutable SHA, Stress workflow_dispatch id/headSha, every OS/job/row checkout SHA/count/cpu/tags/pattern/status, downloaded log paths/digests; independent Network gates netem job id and both first/retry outcome transcripts/receipt validity/restoration, including unavailable timeout fields and accepted-flaky label |
| Scoped security re-confirmation | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/security-review/report.md` | Grok security lane, actual model `claude-grok-4.7[1m]` | Read-only delta from base `8e6319f` to frozen final SHA: changed WireGuard lock/counter/retention/key-cap behavior and changed skip/netem gate/parser/retry logic. Confirm authentication/admission, aggregate LRU/fallback and fail-closed exact exception/outcome handling. **No credential inventory**, no N13 work, no reopening user-only A2/A3, excluded findings or unattributed NB-15/NB-23. Findings name severity, file/line, owner/disposition; original report corrections remain explicit |
| Independent final verification | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/reviews/verify-p48.md` | Sol-fast verifier lane, actual model `claude-gpt-6.1-sol-fast` | Immutable-head verdict and criterion-by-criterion evidence audit; independently confirm source, labels, counts, allowlists, marker/message/signature hygiene, docs/source agreement and all contexts/hosted receipts; fresh gates and focused checks described below |
| Verifier's fresh/focused logs and manifest | `.omc/artifacts/p48-session/reviews/evidence/` and its `SHA256SUMS` | Sol-fast verifier lane | Exact commands/SHAs/hosts/exit codes/tallies for its own executions, overlays and summary; separate audit-from-receipts entries for expensive counts not repeated |

The verifier explicitly performs: (1) source/HEAD/checkout immutability confirmation; (2) independent source inspection of demux unlock/counter/log fields, LRU aggregate bound/fallback/admission, peer-count-before-allocation, DNS retained-entry behavior, H3 unchanged production, sampler held-tail acknowledgment and negative sensitivity, and exact skip/retry classifiers; (3) allowlist-set reconciliation with the named 26+privilege+two opt-in+three capability entries, including wrong-name/package/message negative cases; (4) signed message width, no plan markers in added source/comments/workflows/harness/messages, no new skip/stub/TODO and model/ownership checks; (5) **one fresh full-tree race** and **one fresh non-race proxy-integration gate** at frozen final SHA using the command-matrix budgets; (6) focused demux parent-red/tip, bounded-router/policy-red, H3 historical-red/current-parent/tip and sampler/helper/script sensitivity checks in detached overlays with production unmodified; (7) audit exact accepted counts/CPU/tags/patterns/failures/N, complete hosted rows including N14 rows1/3/4, all 18 required contexts, and retained first/retry failure logs, phase validity, cleanup restoration and accepted-flaky labels; (8) confirm compat/current-plan/docs match implemented source and stated user boundaries.

The verifier may audit the costly count50/count200/count600 campaigns from independently checksum-verified lane/hosted receipts instead of repeating them; each such criterion must say **“audit-from-receipts; not repeated by verifier”** and name the commands/counts/receipt/digest. Its own focused smoke or one-shot sensitivity run is labelled separately, never substituted for acceptance counts. For focused parent/tip/sensitivity executions use explicit `-timeout=30m` and a 35m admitted-shell budget unless the command matrix already names a larger one; preserved tags/cpu/settings apply. A green historical receipt cannot prove a new immutable head by itself.

Every failed, missing or unproven criterion is entered in `rulings-p48.md` with an owner, next action, or a clearly identified explicit lead/user waiver and its evidence boundary; silently converting “unproven” to PASS is forbidden. Security report is not replaced by verifier PASS, nor vice versa. A fix after either review, required-context change after final readback, or any changed source/workflow/docs SHA invalidates the affected immutable-head approvals/receipts: re-freeze, repeat the affected review/focused verification and required/hosted evidence, and obtain new explicit final verdicts before completion. Unaffected reviewed conclusions may be carried only with an explicit unchanged-scope audit, never a silent claim that the old approval names the new SHA.
